SCHEDULE-I 


[See sub-rule () of rule 4] 
Survey and Certification of Cargo Vessels 
l. Applicability : 


The provisions relating to the survey and certification of cargo vessels shall apply in accordance with the 
following international conventions and instruments, namely:— 


(.) International Convention for the Safety of Life at Sea, 974 (as amended): 
(a) Applicable to all cargo vessels of five hundred gross tonnage (500 GT) and above; and 
(b) the GMDSS (Global Maritime Distress and Safety System) — Radio Communication 
requirements shall apply to all cargo vessels of three hundred gross tonnage (300 GT) and above. 

(2) International Convention for the Prevention of Pollution from Ships, 973, as modified by the 
Protocol of 978: 

(a) Applicable to all cargo vessels of four hundred gross tonnage (400 GT) and above; and 
(b) applicable to oil tankers of one hundred and fifty gross tonnage (50 GT) and above. 

(3) International Convention for the Control and Management of Ships' Ballast Water and Sediments, 
2004 and the International Convention on the Control of Harmful Anti-fouling Systems on Ships: 
Applicable to all cargo vessels of four hundred gross tonnage (400 GT) and above. 

(4) International Convention on Load Lines, !966, as modified by the Protocol of 988: 

Applicable to all vessels having a length of twenty-four (24) metres and above. 


(5) International Convention on Tonnage Measurement of Ships, 969, applicable to all vessel of twenty- 
four (24) metres of length and above, engaged on international voyages and domestic voyages. 


2. Surveys for convention and non-convention vessels, as applicable: 


(i) An initial survey before the cargo vessel is put in service or before the Certificate is issued for 

first time; 

(2) annual survey within three months before or after each anniversary date of the certificate; 

(3) periodical survey within three months before or after each anniversary date of the certificate; 

(4) an intermediate survey within three months before or after the 2nd or 3rd anniversary date of the 
certificate; 

(5) a renewal survey shall be carried out at intervals specified by the Director-General, not exceeding 


five years. If the renewal survey is completed within three months before the expiry of the 
existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for five years from the date of expiry of the 
existing certificate. If the renewal survey is completed after the expiry of the existing certificate, 
the new certificate shall be valid for five years from the date of expiry of the existing certificate. 


If the renewal survey is completed more than three months before the expiry date of the existing 


84 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


certificate, the new certificate shall be valid from the date of completion of the renewal survey to 


a date not exceeding five years from the date of completion of the renewal survey; and 


(6) an additional survey as occasion arises and as decided by the Director-General. 
3. Docking surveys: 
(J) At least two inspections of the vessel’s bottom are required during the 5-year validity of the 


Cargo Ship Safety Construction Certificate or the Cargo Ship Safety Certificate. However, if the 
vessel is not in a port where the survey can be done when the certificate expires, the Director- 
General may extend the certificate's validity. In such cases, the 5-year period for the inspections 
may also be extended to match the extended certificate validity. In all cases, the time between any 


two inspections must not exceed 36 months (3 years). 


(2) In-water surveys may be permitted in lieu of dry-docking for intermediate docking for all vessels 
except Enhanced Survey Programme vessels of more than fifteen years of age, subject to the 
approval of the Director-General and provided that the vessel comply with International Maritime 
Organisation Resolution A.l204(34) — Survey Guidelines under the Harmonized System of 
Survey and Certification (HSSC), 2025 — as well as the applicable requirements of the 


classification society or recognised organisation as may be notified by the Central Government . 
4. Tail shaft surveys: 


All cargo vessels, regardless of size and trading limits, are required to carry out surveys of their tail shafts 
in accordance with the provisions of the Merchant Shipping (Cargo Vessel Construction, Fire Appliances 
and Survey) Rules, 2026. Since the tail shaft forms an integral part of the vessels propulsion and 
machinery system, it is essential to ensure its continued safety and efficiency. The maximum intervals 


between successive tail shaft surveys shall be in accordance with the following schedule: 


Intervals of tail-shaft surveys based on the type of system 


S. No. Type of propulsion system Interval of survey 


l, Shafts with keyed propeller attachments and fitted with continuous liners or oil five years 
glands, or corrosion resistant materials and designed to reduce stress concentration 
and approved by the recognised organisation. 


2; Shafts having keyless-type propeller attachments made of corrosion resistant five years 
materials or fitted with oil glands, approved by the recognised organisation. 

3. Shafts having solid coupling flanges at the after end and fitted with oil glands or five years 
made of corrosion resistant materials, approved by the recognised organisation. 

4. Controllable pitch propellers for main propulsion purposes, provided the system is five years 
approved by the recognised organisation. 

5. Directional propellers, Podded propulsion units, Water jet units, provided the five years 
system is approved by the recognised organisation. 

a All other tail shafts of the propulsion systems not mentioned above, unless two and a half 


specifically approved by the recognised organisation for extended intervals of years 


surveys. 


[भाग ॥-खणूड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 85 


The interval for tail shaft surveys specified in the aforesaid table serves as a broad guideline. Owners or 
managers may alternatively comply with the International Association of Classification Societies JACS) 
Unified Requirement (UR) Z.2, as revised, for the survey, certification, and extension of tail shaft 
surveys. Vessels with approved Tail Shaft Condition Monitoring (TSCM) systems may be granted 
extended intervals up to fifteen years, subject to compliance with recognised organisation -approved 


monitoring procedures. 


5. Boiler surveys: 


Boilers are required to be surveyed periodically in compliance with SOLAS requirements and rules of 
classification societies. A minimum of two internal inspections during each five-year special survey cycle; 
and the interval between any two such internal inspections shall not exceed thirty six months. One of the 
internal inspection in conjunction with Initial or renewal Safety Construction Survey and the other with 
Intermediate Safety Construction Survey. The external or visual inspection shall be conducted annually in 


conjunction with annual or intermediate or renewal or initial survey. 
6. Statutory surveys and certification: 


(J) The surveys specified at paragraph 2 of this Schedule shall be conducted in compliance with the 
respective convention and these rules as applicable for issuance and maintenance of the following 


statutory certificates issued as under: 


(a) International Load Line Certificate; 

(b) Cargo Ship Safety Certificate; 

(c) Cargo Ship Safety Construction Certificate; 

(d) Cargo Ship Safety Equipment Certificate; 

(e) Cargo Ship Safety Radio Certificate; 

(f) Certificate of Fitness for the Carriage of Dangerous Chemicals in Bulk; 


(g) Certificate of Fitness for the Carriage of Liquefied Gases in Bulk; 


(h) International Oil Pollution Prevention Certificate; 

(i) International Pollution Prevention Certificate for the Carriage of Noxious Liquid Substances in 
Bulk; 

GQ) International Sewage Pollution Prevention Certificate; 

(k) International Air Pollution Prevention Certificate; 


(l) International Energy Efficiency Certificate; 


(m) International Tonnage Certificate; 


(n) International Ballast Water Management Certificate; and 
(0) International Anti-Fouling System Certificate; 
(2) The surveys referred at paragraph 2 of this Schedule shall be carried out for the purpose of 


issuing the following certificates and for ensuring their continued validity during the operation of 


86 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


convention vessels intended to operate within the coastal waters and non-convention vessels 
which complies with applicable requirements of rules under the Act, but do not fully comply with 


the requirements of SOLAS 974, as amended: 


(a) Indian Cargo Ship Safety Construction Certificate; 
(b) Indian Cargo Ship Safety Equipment Certificate; and 
(c) Indian Cargo Ship Safety Radio Certificate. 


(3) The surveys referred to in paragraph 2 of this Schedule shall be carried out for the purpose of 
issuing the following certificates, as applicable, for both Convention vessels and non-Convention 
vessels intended to operate within the Coastal Waters which complies with applicable 
requirements of rules under the Act, but do not fully comply with the requirements of 


International Conventions: 


(a) Indian Oil Pollution Prevention Certificate; 

(b) Indian Pollution Prevention Certificate for the Carriage of Noxious Liquid Substance in Bulk; 
(c) Indian Sewage Pollution Prevention Certificate; 

(d) Indian Air Pollution Prevention Certificate; 


(e) Indian Loadline Certificate; 
(f) Indian Tonnage Certificate; 
(g) Indian Anti-Fouling System Certificate; and 


(h) Indian Ballast Water Management Certificate. 
de Approval of plans, drawings, manuals and booklets for issuance of statutory certificates: 


(i) Any plan, drawing, manual or booklet towards the issuance of statutory certification of an 
existing vessel undergoing change of flag to Indian registry, shall be deemed to have been 
approved by the Director-General if such document has been duly approved by any recognised 
organisation notified by the Central Government, on behalf of any other Flag State 
Administration. No further approval from the Director-General or any other recognised 
organisation is required in these cases, provided no substantial deviation from the applicable 
national or international instruments, affecting the safety of lives or protection of the environment 
is reported during the statutory inspection and certification services rendered by the concerned 
recognised organisation. The Registering authority shall appropriately record the reason as above, 
for deeming any plan, drawing, manual or booklets as ‘approved’ by the Director-General. The 
Owners shall ensure such drawings, booklets or manuals are legible and in English language and 


contain all the information required as per relevant instrument. 


(2) Recognised organisation undertaking statutory surveys for a vessel's change of flag to the Indian 


registry shall confirm that all relevant plans, drawings, booklets, and manuals are duly approved 


[भाग ॥-खणूड 3(i)] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 87 


by a recognised organisation notified by the Central Government. These documents shall in 
generally comply with the applicable Rules enacted, international conventions, codes, and any 
guidelines or notices issued by the International Maritime Organisation and the Director-General 


in this regard. 


Such compliance shall be ensured prior to the issuance of the respective statutory certificates. 
Any non-compliance or deficiency identified must be communicated in writing for prompt 


rectification, and in no case shall the rectification period exceed three months. 


(3) In the event of a change of flag, the procedure outlined above shall be followed for the approval 
of plans, drawings, manuals, and booklets related to load lines. Upon satisfactory completion of 
the required surveys, the recognised organisation notified by the Central Government for the said 


purpose shall issue the full-term Load Line Certificate. 


(4) For initial assignment of load line of newly constructed vessels, the recognised organisation 
notified by the Central Government for the said purpose shall approve all the plans, drawings, 
booklets, manuals etc. as required and applicable, and carry out the initial surveys and upon 


satisfactory compliance with all applicable requirements shall issue full term load line certificate. 


(5) Any plan, drawing, manual or booklet related to a newly constructed vessel must be approved by 
a recognised organisation that has been authorised by the Director-General through an order, 
specifically for the purpose of statutory certification services. Each approved document must 
clearly display the name, designation, signature, and date of the authorised person who has 
certified it. If any of these details are missing or illegible, the local representative of the 
concerned recognised organisation shall be responsible for certifying the authenticity and veracity 


of such documents. 


(6) In the event of a change of flag, the recognised organisation undertaking the statutory surveys 
shall issue a Provisional International Tonnage Certificate (ITC) valid for six months, based on 
the International Tonnage Certificate issued under the previous flag. The recognised organisation 
shall forward this provisional certificate, along with the tonnage computations carried out by any 
recognised organisation notified by the Central Government, to the Registrar of the vessel for 
issuance of a permanent International Tonnage Certificate. However, if the tonnage computations 
are not carried out by a recognised organisation and not in accordance with the provisions of the 
International Tonnage Convention, 969 (ITC 969), the recognised organisation authorised by 
the Director-General through an order shall undertake a fresh tonnage measurement in accordance 
with the provisions of the Merchant Shipping (Tonnage Measurement of Vessels) Rules, 2026. 
The revised tonnage computations shall be submitted to the Registrar of the vessel within the 
validity period of the Provisional International Tonnage Certificate for the purpose of issuing the 


permanent I International Tonnage Certificate. 


88 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


(7) For a new construction vessel, the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or the recognised 
organisation notified by the Central Government, shall carry out the tonnage measurement and 
computations in accordance with the provisions of the Merchant Shipping (Tonnage 
Measurement of Vessels) Rules, 2026. Upon completion, the tonnage details shall be submitted to 
the Registrar of the vessel for the issuance of the Permanent International Tonnage Certificate 


(ITC) or Indian Tonnage Certificate, as applicable. 


8. Exemptions: 


(i) When acquiring vessels that are not newly built, any exemptions granted by the previous Flag 
State Administration in accordance with the relevant international conventions shall remain valid 
for a period of three months, or until the original expiration date of such exemptions under the 
previous flag, whichever occurs earlier and the recognised organisation shall issue exemption 
accordingly with an intimation to the Director-General. If an extension of the validity of such 
exemptions is required beyond this period, the owner or manager of the vessel shall apply to the 
Director-General within the current validity period. The application must be accompanied by the 
recommendations of the recognised organisation and include the applicable fee as specified in the 
schedule. For the continued validity of existing exemptions granted by the previous flag and in 
accordance with the relevant international conventions, the recognised organisation shall forward 
the details to the Director-General for concurrence, enabling the Director-General or recognised 


organisation to issue the exemptions under Indian flag. 


(2) For newly built vessels, any exemption to be granted in accordance with the relevant international 
conventions for the issuance of statutory certificates shall require the owners or managers of such 
vessels to submit an application to the Director-General. The application shall be accompanied by 
the recognised organisation recommendations and the applicable fee as specified in the schedule. 
Such requests will be considered in line with the provisions of the relevant international 
conventions. 


Conducting surveys: 


(i) All vessel owners or managers shall submit applications for all applicable statutory surveys to the 
jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or the recognised organisation notified by the 
Central Government, well within the designated window period. This is to ensure timely 


planning, execution of surveys, and issuance of statutory certificates. 


(2) The renewal Load line survey and issuance of full term Load Line Certificate shall be undertaken 
by a recognised organisation notified by the Central Government for the said purpose. The annual 
Load line survey can be undertaken by any one of the ROs of the Central Government. On 
satisfactory completion of annual Load line survey, the recognised organisation may endorse the 
load line certificate towards completion of annual Load line survey. Further, the concerned 


recognised organisation should ensure that the reports of the annual Load line surveys are 


[भाग ॥-खणूड 3(i)] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 89 


expeditiously forwarded to a recognised organisation authorised by the Director-General for 


updating the records of Load line surveys. 


0. Supplementary surveys by the Administration: 


(J) International Maritime Organisation Resolution A.739 (8) read with the applicable recognised 
organisation Code requires a Flag State Administration to establish, implement, and maintain an 
oversight programme over recognised organisations. This oversight programme shall include 
supplementary surveys (undertaken by the Administration itself) to ensure that vessels entitled to 
fly its flag actually comply with the requirements of applicable international Maritime 
Organisation instruments and vessels also comply with national requirements (which may extend 


or supplement the International Maritime Organisation mandatory requirements). 


(2) The periodic surveys of Cargo Ship Safety Equipment Certificate of all Indian vessels while in 
India shall be undertaken exclusively by the surveyors of the Mercantile Marine Departments, 
provided that the survey and certification of the vessel are carried out exclusively by the 
recognised organisation, unless the concerned Principal Officer considers it necessary to delegate 
this survey to a recognised organisation in lieu thereof, on a case to case basis, on account of 
specific exigencies. Considering that such surveys fall only once in the five year survey cycle of 
the vessel and has the flexibility to align either with the second or third annual survey, the vessel 
owners or managers shall be advised to plan the surveys well in advance and approach the 
jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Departments accordingly. Further, the attending surveyors of 
Mercantile Marine Department, along with the survey referred above, may undertake Flag State 


Inspection (FSI) of the vessel, mandatorily. 


(3) Above referred surveys can be undertaken by any of the recognised organisations authorised for 
the purpose while the vessel is trading exclusively in foreign waters during the window period for 


the above referred surveys, unless the Director-General advises otherwise in specific cases. 


(4) All other statutory surveys shall be undertaken by any of the recognised organisation notified by 
the Central Government, at the choice of the concerned vessel owner or manager. Provided that, 
the renewal surveys of the Safety Construction (SAFCON), Safety Radio (SRT) and International 
Convention for the Prevention of Pollution from Ships (MARPOL) Annex-I (Prevention of 
Pollution by Oil) of Indian vessel while being undertaken in India shall have the compulsory 
participation of a surveyor from the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Departments. The respective 
recognised organisation undertaking the above referred surveys in India shall intimate the 
jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department well in advance, to facilitate participation of the 
surveyors in such surveys, subject to case by case waiver by the concerned Principal officer on 


account of specific exigencies. 


(5) The owners shall arrange the necessary logistic support to the attending surveyors, but no 
additional fee is to be paid, except the visit fees for the participation of the survey. The surveyors 


from the Mercantile Marine Departments, subsequent to attending of such surveys shall make 


90 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


ll. 


2. 


necessary endorsement in the relevant ‘survey report’ to enable the renewal of the respective 
certificate by the concerned recognised organisation, without any further approval of the Director- 


General. 


Over-due statutory surveys: 


The owners or managers of a cargo vessel shall apply to the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department 
or a recognised organisation to conduct the required surveys within the designated window period. 
However, if the owners or managers intend to carry out the surveys outside the specified window period, 
or intend to commence surveys if the surveys are overdue, shall obtain prior concurrence from the 


Director-General for conducting such overdue surveys beyond the allowed timeframe. 


Extension of dry docking, tail shaft, mandatory statutory surveys and certificates: 


(]) No statutory certificate shall be extended beyond its validity for the purpose of commercial 
operation. However, if a vessel is not in the port where the survey is to be conducted at the time 
the certificate expires, the Director-General may extend the validity of the certificate for a 
maximum period of three months. This extension is granted only to allow the vessel to complete 


its voyage to the port where the survey will be carried out. 


(2) In exceptional circumstances, the extension of the renewal and / or docking survey shall be 
granted only in accordance with the provisions of the Safety of Life At Sea, Load Line, 
International Convention for the Prevention of Pollution from Ships (MARPOL) or any other 
applicable Convention, as well as the applicable rules under the Act, including non-convention 
vessels. Such applications shall be submitted by the top management under the International 


Safety Management Code or by the Designated Person Ashore (DPA) of the company. 


(3) The Director-General may not grant surveys extension for commercial reasons or due to 
insufficient advance planning. To request an extension, vessel owners or managers must provide 
clear documentary evidence demonstrating diligent and timely planning efforts. Generic 
correspondence, such as routine dry-dock booking letters, will not suffice. If the Director-General 
is satisfied that the owners took all necessary steps to secure a dry dock but were unable to do so 


for reasons beyond their control, a one-month extension may be considered, subject to: 


(a) satisfactory afloat surveys by the Classification Society; 

(b) class certification of the vessel’s seaworthiness for the extension period; and 

(c) in exceptional cases, an additional period up to two months may be granted for following: 
(i) an underwater closed circuit television (CCTV) survey in the presence of class or 


recognised organisation, 
(ii) review of progress of other afloat surveys, and 


(iii) evidence of continued efforts to obtain dry-docking, either in India or abroad. 


[भाग ॥-खणूड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 92 


(4) Approval of an extension is not guaranteed, and the Director-General reserves the right to deny 


requests if planning is deemed inadequate or if the vessel's condition is unsatisfactory. 


(5) In order to facilitate the expeditious processing of survey extensions, cargo vessel (excluding 
offshore supply vessels) that are not more than twenty years of age, and offshore supply vessels 
(OSVs) that are not more than fifteen years of age, shall be eligible for this process, subject to the 


following conditions: 


(a) the vessel must not have any pending or overdue class or statutory surveys and no outstanding 


class recommendations or conditions affecting certification; 


(b) the vessel’s performance and maintenance history during the current certification period must be 
satisfactory; 
(c) the vessel must not have undergone any significant modification or damage repair that would 


require additional survey; 


(d) the vessel must have no more than three Condition of Classes (CoCs), and none of those 


Condition of Class should have been previously extended; 


(e) there should be no major Non Conformities in the last three consecutive Safety Management 
Certificate (SMC) audits of the vessel and Document of Compliance (DOC) audits of the 


company; 


(f) the company should not have requested extensions for Port State Control (PSC) or Flag State 
Inspection (FSI) deficiencies and the vessel is not detained under any Memorandum of 


Understanding (MoU) or during an Flag State Inspection (FSJ) in the current certification period; 


(g) the vessel must not have been involved in any marine casualty; and 
(h) the maximum permissible period of thirty-six months between two dockings shall not be 
exceeded. 


The owners or managers of such vessels shall submit an application, along with all supporting documents 
that comply with the above-mentioned requirements, to a recognised organisation that has been authorised 
by the Director-General through an order, specifically for the purpose. The recognised organisation shall 
consider the extension of surveys — including the docking survey and tail shaft survey — for a period of 
one month and forward its report to the Director-General for consideration of a further extension of up to 
two months, but not exceeding a total of three months from the date of expiry of the existing full-term 
certificates. However, the extension of surveys beyond one month, the vessel required to undergo under 
water inspection of hull and its appendages, propeller and rudder by a satisfactory closed circuit television 


(CCTV) inspection by the recognised organisation.

Schedule-I 


[See sub-rule () of rule 4] 


Survey and Certification of Passenger Ships 


8 Applicability.- 
The provisions of this Schedule shall apply to the following classes of passenger ships, 


categorised in accordance with the nature and extent of their voyages: 
(i) Class I - Passenger Ships engaged on international voyages other than ships of Class III. 
(2) Class II - Passenger Ships engaged on short international voyages other than ships of Class IV. 
(3) Class III - Special Trade Passenger Ships engaged on international voyages. 
(4) Class IV - Special Trade Passenger Ships engaged on short international voyages. 


(5) Class V - Special Trade Passenger Ships (other than ships of Class VI) engaged on voyages other than 


international voyages. 


(6) Class VI - Special Trade Passenger Ships engaged on voyages in the coasting trade of India during the 
course of which they do not go more than 20 miles from the nearest land. Provided that such ships shall 
not cease to be ships of Class VI merely by reason of the fact that they cross during their voyage the 
Gulf of Kutch, Cambay or Mannar. 


2. Surveys: 


2.I. Safety of Life at Sea (SOLAS) for both passenger and special trade passenger ships surveys: 


(i) an initial survey before the passenger ship is put in service or before the certificate is issued for the first 
time; 
(2) a renewal survey at intervals specified by the Director-General but not exceeding twelve months except 


where the renewal survey is completed within three months before the expiry date of the existing 
certificate, the new certificate shall be valid from the date of completion of the renewal survey to a date 
not exceeding twelve months from the date of expiry of the existing certificate or the renewal survey is 
completed after the expiry date of the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid from the date 
of completion of renewal survey to a date not exceeding twelve months from the date of expiry of the 
existing certificate or when the renewal survey is completed more than three months before the expiry 


date of the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid from the date of completion of the 


[भाग ॥--खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 


207 


(3) 


2.2. 


() 


(2) 


(3) 


renewal survey to a date not exceeding twelve months from the date of completion of renewal survey, 


and; 
an additional survey as occasion arises and as decided by the Director-General. 


The Surveys referred above shall be carried out as follows for issuance of Passenger Ship Safety 


Certificate as the case may be: 


the initial survey shall include a complete inspection of the Ship’s structure, machinery and equipment, 
including the outside of the ship's bottom and the inside and outside of the boilers. This survey shall be 
such as to ensure that the arrangements, materials and scantlings of the structure, boilers and other 
pressure ships and their appurtenances, main and auxiliary machinery, electrical installation, radio 
installations including those used in life-saving appliances, fire protection, fire safety systems and 
appliances, life-saving appliances and arrangements, ship-borne navigational equipment, nautical 
publications, means of embarkation for pilots and other equipment fully comply with the requirements 
of the present regulations, and of the laws, orders and regulations promulgated as a result thereof by the 
Director-General for ships of the service for which it is intended. The survey shall also be such as to 
ensure that the workmanship of all parts of the ship and its equipment is in all respects satisfactory, and 
that the ship is provided with the lights, shapes, means of making sound signals and distress signals as 
required by the provisions of the present regulations under the Safety of Life at Sea (SOLAS) and the 


International Regulations for Preventing Collisions at Sea in force; 


the renewal survey shall include an inspection of the structure, boilers and other pressure ships, 
machinery and equipment, including the outside of the ship's bottom. The survey shall be such as to 
ensure that the Ship, as regards the structure, boilers and other pressure ships and their appurtenances, 
main and auxiliary machinery, electrical installation, radio installations including those used in life- 
saving appliances, fire protection, fire safety systems and appliances, life-saving appliances and 
arrangements, ship-borne navigational equipment, nautical publications, means of embarkation for pilots 
and other equipment is in satisfactory condition and is fit for the service for which it is intended, and that 
it complies with the requirements of the present regulations and of the laws, orders and regulations 
promulgated as a result thereof by the Director-General. The lights, shapes, means of making sound 
signals and distress signals carried by the ship shall also be subject to the abovementioned survey for the 
purpose of ensuring that they comply with the requirements of the present regulations under SOLAS and 


of the International Regulation for Preventing Collisions at Sea in force; 


an additional survey either general or partial, according to the circumstances, shall be made after a repair 


resulting from investigations prescribed in regulation l of SOLAS Chapter I, or whenever any 


208 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


important repairs or renewals are made. The survey shall be such as to ensure that the necessary repairs 
or renewals have been effectively made, that the material and workmanship of such repairs or renewals 
are in all respects satisfactory, and that the ship complies in all respects with the provisions of the 
present regulations under SOLAS and of the International Regulations for Preventing Collisions at Sea 


in force, and of the laws, orders and regulations promulgated as a result thereof by the Director-General; 
2.3. Hull and Machinery Surveys: 


(i) The Hull and Machinery of any passenger ship shall be surveyed in accordance with the principle of 
‘Continuous Survey of Hull and Continuous Survey of Machinery (CSH & CSM) scheme, and shall be 
opened up and surveyed within the period of five years. For this purpose, a proper schedule of surveys 


needs to be drawn up by the ship-owner and approved by the Director-General. 


(2) The Classification Society or Recognised Organisation (RO), who shall also draw-up similar schedule 
for the periodical survey as referred above to avoid duplication of surveys, of these items under the 
‘Continuous Survey of Hull and Continuous Survey of Machinery’ (CSH and CSM) scheme in a five 


year cycle. 


(3) For the purpose of issuance of Passenger Ship Safety Certificate, the owners shall submit the survey 
plan drawn up as above to the concerned Mercantile Marine Department at the commencement of the 
respective annual cycle, who will- then specify the items which may require personal attendance of the 
Mercantile Marine Department surveyor. The owner shall facilitate the attendance of the surveyor(s) of 
the Mercantile Marine Department in such cases. However, all other items surveyed and authenticated 
by the surveyors of the respective recognised organisation shall be credited for the purpose of the 
‘Continuous Survey of Hull and Continuous Survey of Machinery (CSH and CSM) scheme, provided the 
Principal Officer is satisfied with the same. The overall responsibility for confirming the effective 
implementation of the "'Continuous Survey of Hull and Continuous Survey of Machinery (CSH and 
CSM) scheme, however, lies with the concerned Principal Officer who issues the Passenger Ship Safety 


Certificate. 
2.4. Tail shaft and boiler surveys: 
(J) Tail shaft survey: 


All passenger ships, regardless of size and trading limits, are required to carry out surveys of their tail 
shafts in accordance with the provisions of the Merchant Shipping (Passenger Ship Construction, Fire 
Appliances and Survey) Rules, 2026. Since the tail shaft forms an integral part of the ships propulsion 
and machinery system, it is essential to ensure its continued safety and efficiency. The maximum 


intervals between successive tail shaft surveys shall be in accordance with the following schedule:

survey 
l. Shafts with keyed propeller attachments and fitted with continuous five years 


liners or oil glands, or corrosion resistant materials and designed to 
reduce stress concentration and approved by the recognised 
organisation. 


2: Shafts having keyless-type propeller attachments made of corrosion five years 
resistant materials or fitted with oil glands, approved by the 
recognised organisation. 


3. Shafts having solid coupling flanges at the after end and fitted with five years 
oil glands or made of corrosion resistant materials, approved by the 
recognised organisation. 


4. Controllable pitch propellers for main propulsion purposes, provided five years 
the system is approved by the recognised organisation. 


2. Directional propellers, Podded propulsion units, Water jet units, five years 
provided the system is approved by the recognised organisation. 


|| All other tail shafts of the propulsion systems not mentioned above, two and half 
unless specifically approved by the recognised organisation for

extended intervals of surveys. 


The interval for tail shaft surveys specified in the aforesaid table serves as a broad guideline. Owners or 
managers may alternatively comply with the International Association of Classification Societies (IACS) 
Unified Requirement (UR) Z.2l, as revised, for the survey, certification, and extension of tail shaft 
surveys. Ships with approved Tail Shaft Condition Monitoring (TSCM) systems may be granted 
extended intervals up to ten years, subject to compliance with recognised organisation-approved 


monitoring procedures. 
(2) Boiler surveys: 


Boilers are required to be surveyed periodically in compliance with SOLAS requirements and the rules 
of the classification societies. A minimum of two internal inspections during each five-year special 
survey cycle; and the interval between any two such internal inspections shall not exceed thirty-six 
months. The internal inspection in conjunction with Initial or renewal-Survey-of Passenger Ship Safety 
Certificate. The external or visual inspections shall be conducted annually in conjunction with initial or 


renewal survey up to the age of twenty-five years. Beyond twenty-five years of age the boilers are 


20 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


required to undergo internal inspection once in a year in conjunction with renewal survey of Passenger 


Ship Safety Certificate. 
2:3: Frequency of dry-docking surveys 


(i) Any passenger ship, other than ro-ro passenger Ship, of less than fifteen years of age, shall undergo a 
minimum of one of the inspections of the outside of the ship's bottom during any five-year period in dry- 
dock. In all such cases, the maximum interval between any two dry-dock bottom inspections shall not 


exceed sixty months. 


(2) Any passenger ship, other than ro-ro passenger Ship, of above fifteen years of age but less than thirty 
years of age, shall undergo a not less than minimum of two of the inspections of the outside of the ship's 
bottom during any five-year period in dry-dock. In all such cases, the maximum interval between any 


two dry-dock bottom inspections should not exceed thirty six months. 


(3) Any passenger Ship, other than ro-ro passenger ship, which is above thirty years of age, shall undergo 
the inspection of the outside of the ship's bottom in dry-dock during every alternate Annual Passenger- 


ship Survey (APS). 


(4) | Where inspections of the Ship’s bottom required for the renewal survey are not conducted in dry-dock 
such inspection may be carried out with the ship afloat through an effective closed circuit television 
(CCTV) underwater examination. In all such cases, along with closed circuit television under-water 
inspection, a General Inspection (GJ) of the ship, with particular emphasis on the structural fitness of the 
ship, covering the accessible internal areas as far as practically possible, shall be conducted by the 


recognised organisation and Mercantile Marine Department. 


(5) All bottom inspection, regardless of the method, shall be carried out within the allowable window period 
for the Passenger Ship Safety Certificate renewal survey (i.e., within the three months window before 


the expiry date of the certificate), as the case may be. 


(6) Notwithstanding the foregoing provisions, a bottom inspection in dry-dock shall, in all cases, be carried 
out within the fifteen month period between the fourth annual survey and the renewal survey of the 


International Load Line Certificate 
2.6. Conditions for in-water survey in lieu of dry-dock: 


(i) In-water survey in lieu of dry-docking is permitted only for Ships which fully meet the conditions of 
Maritime Safety Committee.l/Circ.l348 (Guidelines for the assessment of technical provisions for the 


performance of an in-water survey in lieu of bottom inspection in dry-dock to permit one dry-dock 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 


2Ii 


(2) 


(3) 


(4) 


(5) 


(6) 


(7) 


(8) 


examination in any five-year period for passenger ships other than ro-ro passenger ships) and assigned 


with suitable In-water survey (IWS) notation or equivalent, by the concerned recognised organisation. 


Inspections of the outside of the Ship’s bottom conducted afloat should only be carried out through an 
under-water surveying agency approved for the purpose by the respective recognised organisation and 


when the conditions are satisfactory and proper equipment and suitably qualified staff is available. 


The concermed recognised organisation shall review the ship survey records to confirm that the current 
condition of hull and machinery and the hull protection system is suitable to accomplish the In-water 
Survey in accordance with the recommendations specified in Maritime Safety Committee. l/Circ.348. 
There shall not be any overdue condition of class (CoC), recommendation, or similar note relating to a 


defect in any part of the Ship’s structure or the Ship’s bottom, particularly those below the water line. 


का all such cases, where the owner or the company opts for In-water survey in lieu of dry-docking, the 
Master of the ship shall confirm in writing that the ship has not sustained any grounding or contact 
damage and that nothing unusual has been observed to suspect that any part of the ship's bottom or 


protuberances has been damaged or breached in any manner, since the previous bottom inspection. 


A basic requirement for consideration to allow in-water survey in-lieu of dry-dock is that a 
comprehensive maintenance regime based upon a five-year cycle as mentioned under sub-paragraph (6) 
is effectively implemented by the company for the relevant items. In particular, the relevant maintenance 
considerations as indicated in paragraph 5 of Maritime Safety Committee. /Circ.l348 must be taken into 


account by the owner, the recognised organisation, and the Director-General as the case may be. 


In all such cases, the design life of components, manufacturer's recommended maintenance, company's 
implemented ship's maintenance system and classification society survey requirements should not be in 
conflict with the bottom inspection of passenger ships when the inspection is intended to be carried out 


through In-water Survey in-lieu of dry-dock. 


The in-water inspection is to be performed under the direct supervision and to the satisfaction of the 
attending recognised organisation surveyor(s) and shall include video recording of the Ship’s hull by the 


diver. 


The Principal Officer, Mercantile Marine Department who is intending to issue the Passenger Ship 
Safety Certificate, to the ship shall over-see the in-water survey through nominated surveyor(s) and may 
accept the same in-lieu of dry-dock surveys for the purpose of issuance of the respective certificate(s), 


subject to his satisfaction with regard to the in-water survey effectiveness and its results. 


2i2 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


2.7. Validity of Indian Passenger Ship Certificates and Special Trade Indian Passenger Ship 


Certificates: 


(i) Passenger Ship Safety Certificate for Indian passenger Ships shall have maximum validity of twelve 
months from the anniversary date, which shall be aligned with the anniversary date of the International 
Load Line certificate. In cases where the dry-docking or In-water survey of the ship is not falling due 
during the window period of the certificate, the certificate may still be renewed for maximum period of 
twelve months, aligning with the anniversary date of the International Load Line certificate, provided 
that all the items of the continuous survey of hull and machinery under the respective cycle, except those 
requiring dry-docking, has been satisfactorily surveyed prior to the issuance of the certificate. The 
certificate shall have a clear mention of the next due date of the dry-dock or In-water survey and shall 
cease to be valid if a survey in dry-dock or in-water survey in-lieu of such dry-docking, is not completed 


within the maximum intervals referred under paragraph 2.5 above. 


(2) Further, the Passenger Ship Safety Certificate for Indian Special Trade Passenger (STP) Ships operating 
exclusively in the coast of India may be issued for a maximum period of twelve months from the date of 
last inspection of the hull and fittings of such ship in a dry-dock (date of un-docking survey), under 
special circumstances, by the concerned Principal Officer. The special circumstances in this case are 
where the Ship has been laid-up or has been out of service for a considerable period because of a major 
repair or modification, but does not include any previous extension granted to the certificate. However, 
no extension to the Passenger Ship Safety Certificate for STP ships, further to the twelve months from 
the date of undocking survey, shall be permitted in such cases and the validity of the certificate shall be 
brought in alignment with the 'anniversary date’ of the Load line certificate by the next scheduled 


Annual Passenger Ship Survey (APS). 
2.8. Other statutory surveys as applicable: 


The survey and certification of Passenger ships and Special Trade Passenger Ships under other 
International Maritime Organisation conventions, such as International Convention for Prevention of 
Pollution from Ships (MARPOL), International Tonnage Convention, 969 (ITC-l969), Load Line 
Convention, the Ballast Water Management Convention, the Anti-Fouling System Convention and any 


other conventions as applicable, must be carried as detailed: 


(i) an initial survey before the cargo ship is put in service or before the Certificate is issued for first 
time; 
(2) an annual survey within three months before or after each anniversary date of the certificate as 


applicable and indicated in the table under paragraph 8; 


[भाग ॥--खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 


23 


2.9. 


2.0. 


() 


(3) an intermediate survey within three months before or after the second or third anniversary date 


of the Certificate as applicable and indicated in the table under paragraph 8; 


(4) a renewal survey at intervals specified by the Director-General but not exceeding five years 
except where the renewal Survey is completed within three months before the expiry date of the 
existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid from the date of completion of the renewal 
survey to a date not exceeding five years from the date of expiry of the existing certificate or the 
renewal survey is completed after the expiry date of the existing certificate, the new certificate 
shall be valid from the date of completion of renewal survey to a date not exceeding five years 
from the date of expiry of the existing certificate or when the renewal survey is completed more 
than three months before the expiry date of the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be 
valid from the date of completion of the renewal survey to a date not exceeding five years from 


the date of completion of the renewal survey, and; 
(5) an additional survey as occasion arises and as decided by the Director-General. 


The anniversary date of all statutory certificates of a Passenger Ship or Special Trade Passenger Ship, 
hereinafter, should be aligned with the 'anniversary date’ of the International Load Line Certificate as the 


case may be. 


All passenger ship owners or managers must submit applications for the applicable statutory surveys to 
the jurisdictional Principal Officer, Mercantile Marine Department well within the prescribed window 
period. This is essential to enable timely planning and execution of the surveys, as well as the issuance 
of statutory certificates unless the responsibility for these surveys has been specifically delegated to a 
recognised organisation. However, for Load Line surveys and certification, owners are required to 


directly approach the recognised organisation notified by the Central Government for the said purpose. 


Over-due statutory surveys: Owners or managers of passenger Ships shall apply to the jurisdictional 
Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation notified by the Central Government to 
conduct statutory surveys within the designated window period. If owners or managers of the passenger 
ship is intend to carry out the surveys outside the window period or if the surveys are already overdue, 


shall obtain prior approval from the Director-General before proceeding to offer the ship for surveys. 
Extension of statutory certificates: 


No statutory certificate to be extended beyond its validity for the purpose of the commercial operation. 
However, a passenger ship at the time when a certificate expires is not in a port in which it is to be 


surveyed, the Director-General may extend the period of validity of the certificate for a maximum period 


24 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


(2) 


of three months only for the purpose of allowing the passenger ship to complete its voyage to the port in 


which it is to be surveyed. 


The Director-General, may grant an extension of the statutory surveys of any special trade passenger 
Ship in the public interest, upon the request of the ship owner or manager. Such an extension shall only 
be considered after due verification, review, and assessment of the ship's condition through a survey, the 
scope of which shall be determined by the Director-General. The survey must be carried out by a 
surveyor from the Administration or a recognised organisation authorised by the Director-General. 
Provided that, the statutory certificate shall not be extended beyond a maximum period of three months 


under any circumstances. 


Approval of plans, drawings, manuals and booklets for issuance of statutory certificates: 


(J) Any plan, drawing, manual or booklet towards the issuance of statutory certification of an 
existing ship undergoing change of flag to India registry, shall be accepted provisionally by the 
Director-General, if the same is duly approved in accordance with the applicable convention on 
behalf of any other Administration by any recognised organisation as notified by the Central 
Government for considering issuance of short term certificates for three months by the 
jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation. The owners or 
managers of such ships must submit all the required plans to a recognised organisation 
authorised by the Director-General through an order within the validity of short term certificates 
for review and submitting the plans to Director-General for review and approval of the same and 
for issuance full term certificates by the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or 
recognised organisation. Owners shall ensure such drawings or booklets or manuals are legible 


and in English language and contain all the information required as per relevant instruments. 


(2) In the event of a change of flag, the procedure outlined above shall be followed for the approval 
of plans, drawings, manuals, and booklets related to load lines. Upon satisfactory completion of 
the required surveys, the recognised organisation notified by the Central Government for the 


said purpose shall issue the full-term Load Line Certificate. 


(3) For initial assignment of load line for new construction ships, the recognised organisation 
notified by the Central Government for the said purpose shall approve all the plans, drawings, 
booklets, manuals etc. as required and applicable, and carry out the initial surveys towards 


issuance of full term load line certificate. 


(4) Any plan, drawing, or booklet related to a newly constructed ship must be reviewed by a 


recognised organisation that has been authorised by the Director-General through an order in 


[भाग ॥--खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 


2I5 


(5) 


(6) 


accordance with applicable rules under the Act and Conventions, specifically for the purpose of 
statutory certification services and forwarding the plans to Director-General for review and 


approval. 


In the event of a change of flag, the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or recognised 
organisation undertaking the statutory surveys shall issue a Provisional International Tonnage 
Certificate (ITC) valid for six months, based on the International Tonnage Certificate issued 
under the previous flag. The jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or recognised 
organisation shall forward this provisional certificate, along with the tonnage computations 
carried out by any recognised organisation notified by the Central Government, to the Registrar 
of the ship for issuance of a permanent International Tonnage Certificate. However, if the 
tonnage computations were not carried out by a recognised organisation notified by the Central 
Government and not in accordance with the provisions of the International Tonnage Convention, 
969 (ITC-969), the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation 
authorised by the Director-General through an order shall undertake a fresh tonnage 
measurement in accordance with the provisions of the Merchant Shipping (Tonnage 
Measurement of Vessels) Rules, 2026. The revised tonnage computations shall be submitted to 
the Registrar of the ship within the validity period of the Provisional International Tonnage 


Certificate for the purpose of issuing the permanent International Tonnage Certificate. 


For a new construction ship, the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or the recognised 
organisation, as authorised by the Director-General through an order, shall carry out the tonnage 
measurement and computations in accordance with the provisions of the Merchant Shipping 
(Tonnage Measurement of Vessels) Rules, 2026. Upon completion, the tonnage details shall be 
submitted to the Registrar of the ship for the issuance of the Permanent International Tonnage 


Certificate (ITC) or Indian Tonnage Certificate, as applicable. 


Exemptions: 

For any existing or newly built passenger ship or special trade passenger ship, any exemption to 
be granted in accordance with the relevant international conventions or the provisions of the 
rules under the Merchant Shipping Act, 2025 (24 of 2025) for the issuance of statutory 
certificates shall require the owners or managers of such ships to submit an application to the 
Director-General. The application shall be accompanied by the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine 


Department or recognised organisation’s recommendations and the applicable fee as prescribed 


26 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


in the schedule. Such requests will be considered in line with the provisions of the relevant 


international conventions. 


7. Declaration by Master: 


All Special Trade Passenger Ship owners, managers, or agents shall submit a Master’s Declaration in the 
prescribed format (Form-I), duly signed by the Master of the ship, to the jurisdictional Mercantile 
Marine Department (Mercantile Marine Department) at least two hours prior to departure from the port, 
confirming that the Special Trade Passenger Ship complies with the requirements of section |23 of the 


Merchant Shipping Act, 2025 (24 of 2025).

Schedule-HT 


[See sub-rule () of rule 4] 
Survey and Certification of High Speed Crafts. 


l. Applicability: 
The provision contained in this schedule shall apply to; 


(.) Passenger High Speed Crafts; and 
(2) Cargo Crafts of five hundred gross tonnage (500 GT) and above. 


2. Definition: 


High Speed Craft : "High-speed craft" is a craft capable of maximum speed, in meters per second (m/s), equal 


0.667 
V 


to or exceeding: 3.7 where: V = volume of displacement corresponding to the design waterline (m3) 


excluding craft the hull of which is supported completely clear above the water surface in non-displacement 


mode by aerodynamic forces generated by ground effect. 
3. Survey and certification under the High Speed Craft Code: 
(.) Each craft shall be subject to the surveys specified below: 


(i) an initial survey before the craft is put in service or before the Certificate is issued for the first 
time. 

(ii) A renewal survey at intervals specified by the Director-General but not exceeding five years. 
If the renewal survey is completed within three months before the expiry of the existing 
certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for five years from the date of expiry of the 
existing certificate. If the renewal survey is completed after the expiry of the existing 
certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for five years from the date of expiry of the 
existing certificate. If the renewal survey is completed more than three months before the 
expiry date of the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid from the date of 
completion of the renewal survey to a date not exceeding five years from the date of 
completion of the renewal survey, and; 

(iii) a periodical survey within three months before or after each anniversary date of the 
certificate; and 

(iv) an additional survey, either general or partial according to the circumstance, shall be made 
after a repair resulting from an incident occurs to a craft or a defect discovered either of 
which affects the safety of the craft or the efficiency or completeness of structure, equipment, 


fittings, arrangements and materials, and as decided by the Director-General. 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3(i)] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 223 


(2) The initial survey shall include:- 
(i) An appraisal of the assumptions made and limitations proposed in relation to loadings, 
environment, speed and manoeuvrability; 
(ii) An appraisal of the data supporting the safety of the design, obtain, as appropriate, from 
calculations, test and trials; 
(iii) A failure mode and effect analysis as required by the High Speed Craft (HSC) Code; 
(iv) An investigation into the adequacy of the various manuals to be supplied with the craft; and 
(v) A complete inspection of the structure, safety equipment, radio installations and other 
equipment, fittings, arrangements and materials to ensure that they comply with the 
requirements of the High Speed Craft Code are in satisfactory condition and are fit for the 
service for which the craft is intended. 

(3) The renewal and periodical surveys shall include a complete inspection of the structure, including 
the outside of the craft’s bottom and related items, safety equipment, radio installations and other 
equipment as surveyed during initial survey to ensure that they comply with the requirements of 
the High Speed Craft Code are in satisfactory condition for the intended service. 


(4 


a 


The inspection of the craft’s bottom shall be conducted with the craft out of the water under 
suitable conditions for close-up examination of any damaged or problem areas. 


(5 


wm 


The jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or a recognised organisation (RO) notified by 
the Central Government shall conduct the applicable surveys within the designated window 
period. If the craft owners or managers are intend to initiate the surveys outside the window 
period or if the statutory surveys are overdue, shall obtain prior approval from the Directorate 
General before commencing such surveys. 


(6 


wm 


The jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation notified by the 
Central Government may issue a High-Speed Craft Safety Certificate for a maximum period of 
five years from the date of expiry of existing full term certificate or endorse the certificate upon 
satisfactory completion of the survey, as applicable. However, the validity of the High-Speed 
Craft Safety Certificate may be limited to the validity of the craft’s class certificate, or it may 
coincide with the survey cycle of the classification society or recognised organisation, subject to 
compliance of all the High Speed Craft Code requirements as applicable. 

(7) All passenger High-Speed Craft (HSC) surveys and certifications shall be carried out exclusively 
by the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department, unless specific directions are issued by the 


Director-General. 


224 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


4. Permit to operate high speed craft: 


() 


(2) 


(3) 


The craft shall not operate commercially unless a Permit to Operate High Speed Craft is issued 
and valid in addition to the High Speed Craft Safety Certificate. 

The crafts may undertake transit voyages without a valid Permit to Operate High Speed Craft 
provided the craft is not operating commercially with passengers or cargo. However, the owners 
or managers of the craft of shall obtain permission from the Director-General for such voyages. 
The Permit to Operate High Speed Craft shall be issued by the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine 
Department for a maximum period of five years and periodically reviewed and endorsed within 
three months before or after each anniversary date, to certify compliance with the following and 
stipulated conditions of the operation of the craft and be drawn up on the basis of the information 
contained in the route operational manual specified in the Chapter 8 of the High Speed Craft 
Code: 


(i) The management of the company operating the craft exercises strict control over its operation 
and maintenance by a quality management system; 

(ii) The management ensures that only persons qualified to operate the specific type of craft used 
on the intended route are employed; 

(iii) The distance covered and the worst intended conditions in which high speed craft 
operations are permitted will be restricted by the imposition of operational limits; 

(iv) The craft will be at all times be in reasonable proximity to a place of refuge, having due 
regard to the provisions of the High Speed Craft Code; 

(v) Adequate communications facilities, weather forecast and maintenance facilities are available 
within the area of the craft operation; 


(vi) In the intended area of operation, suitable rescue facilities will be readily available. 


5. Route Operational Manual: 


The owner or manager of the High Speed Craft shall submit the Route Operational Manual to the jurisdictional 


Mercantile Marine Department for approval in accordance with the Chapter 8 of the High Speed Craft Code. 


6. Surveys and certification under other International Maritime Organisation Conventions: 


The survey and certification of Passenger or Cargo High-Speed Crafts (HSC) under other International 


Maritime Organisation conventions—such as International Convention for Prevention of Pollution from ships 


(MARPOL), International Tonnage Convention, 969 (ITC-969), Anti-Fouling System Convention and any 


other convention as applicable —must be carried out in accordance with the provisions in the respective rules 


made under the Merchant Shipping Act, 2025 (24 of 2025) and as detailed in the High Speed Craft Code 994 


or 2000, depending on the craft’s certification basis. 


() 
(2) 


An initial survey before the craft is put in service or before the certificate is issued for _ first time; 


an annual survey within three months before or after each anniversary date of the certificate; 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 225 


(3) an intermediate survey within three months before or after the second or third anniversary date of the 
certificate; 
(4) a renewal survey at intervals specified by the Director-General but not exceeding five years. If the 


renewal survey is completed within three months before the expiry of the existing certificate, the new 
certificate shall be valid for five years from the date of expiry of the existing certificate. If the renewal 
survey is completed after the expiry of the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for 
five years from the date of expiry of the existing certificate. If the renewal survey is completed more 
than three months before the expiry date of the existing Certificate, the new Certificate shall be valid 
from the date of completion of the renewal survey to a date not exceeding five years from the date of 
completion of the renewal survey, and; 


(5) an additional survey as occasion arises and as decided by the Director-General. 


(6) All passenger high speed craft owners or managers shall submit applications for the required statutory 
surveys to the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department within the prescribed time window. This ensures 
adequate time for planning and execution of the surveys, as well as for the issuance of the necessary statutory 
certificates — unless responsibility for these surveys has been specifically delegated to recognised 


organisation. 


7. Approval of plans. drawings. manuals and booklets for issuance of statutory certificates: 


() 


(2) 


(3) 


Any plan, drawing, manual or booklet towards the issuance of statutory certification of an existing high speed 
craft undergoing change of flag to India registry, shall be accepted provisionally by the Director-General, if 
the same is duly approved in accordance with the applicable convention on behalf of any other Administration 
by any recognised organisation as notified by the Central Government for considering issuance of short term 
certificates for three months by the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation. 
The owners or managers of such crafts shall submit all the required plans to a recognised organisation 
authorised by the Director-General through an order, within the validity of short term certificates for review 
and submitting the plans to Director-General for review and approval, and for issuance full term certificates 
by the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation. Owners shall ensure such 
drawings or booklets or manuals are legible and in English language and contain all the information required 
as per relevant instruments. 

Any plan, drawing, or booklet related to a newly constructed ship must be reviewed by a recognised 
organisation that has been authorised by the Director-General through an order in accordance with applicable 
rules under the Act and Conventions, specifically for the purpose of statutory certification services and 
forwarding the plans to Director-General for review and approval. 

In the event of a change of flag, the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation 
undertaking the statutory surveys shall issue a provisional International Tonnage Certificate (ITC) valid for six 
months, based on the International Tonnage Certificate issued under the previous flag. The jurisdictional 
Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation shall forward this provisional certificate, along with 


the tonnage computations carried out by any recognised organisation notified by the Central Government, to 


226 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


(4) 


the Registrar of the craft for issuance of a permanent International Tonnage Certificate. However, if the 
tonnage computations were not carried out by a recognised organisation notified by the Central Government 
and not in accordance with the provisions of the International Tonnage Convention, 969 (ITC-969), the 
jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation as authorised by the Director-General 
through an order shall undertake a fresh tonnage measurement in accordance with the provisions of the 
Merchant Shipping (Tonnage Measurement of Vessels) Rules, 2026. The revised tonnage computations shall 
be submitted to the Registrar of the craft within the validity period of the provisional International Tonnage 
Certificate for the purpose of issuing the permanent International Tonnage Certificate. 

For a new construction craft, the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or the recognised organization 
authorised by the Director-General through an order, shall carry out the tonnage measurement and 
computations in accordance with the provisions of the Merchant Shipping (Tonnage Measurement of Vessels) 
Rules, 2026. Upon completion, the tonnage details shall be submitted to the Registrar of the craft for the 
issuance of the Permanent International Tonnage Certificate (ITC) or Indian Tonnage Certificate, as 


applicable. 


8. Formats of certificates and forms: 


(.) All convention certificates shall be issued in the format specified by the organisation. 


(2) All other certificates and forms shall be issued in the format prescribed in the respective rules.
