Schedule-IV 


[See sub-rule () of rule 4] 
Survey and Certification of Mobile Offshore Drilling Units 


l. Applicability: 


The provisions contained in this Schedule shall be applicable to all Mobile Offshore Drilling Units. 


2. Definition: 


Mobile offshore drilling unit (MODU) or unit is a vessel capable of engaging in drilling operations for the 


exploration or for exploitation of resources beneath the seabed such as liquid or gaseous hydrocarbons, 


sulphur or salt, as specified in Code for Construction and equipment of Mobile offshore Drilling Units 


hereinafter referred as Mobile Offshore Drilling Unit (MODU) Code. 


3. Survey and certification under the Mobile Offshore Drilling Unit (MODU) Code: 


l) Each unit should be subject to the surveys specified below: 


(a) 
(b) 


(c 


wma 


(d) 
(९) 


An initial survey before the unit is put in service or before the certificate is issued for the first time; 

a renewal survey at intervals specified by the Director-General but not exceeding five years except 
where the renewal Survey is completed within three months before the expiry date of the existing 
certificate, the new certificate shall be valid from the date of completion of the renewal survey to a 
date not exceeding five years from the date of expiry of the existing certificate or the renewal survey 
is completed after the expiry date of the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for five 
years from the date of expiry of the existing certificate. If the renewal survey is completed more than 
three months before the expiry date of the existing Certificate, the new Certificate shall be valid from 
the date of completion of the renewal survey to a date not exceeding five years from the date of 
completion of the renewal survey; 

an intermediate survey within three months before or after the second anniversary date or within three 
months before or after the third anniversary date of the certificate, which should take the place of one 
of the annual survey; 

an annual survey within three months before or after each anniversary date of the certificate; 

a minimum of two dry-dock surveys during any five-year period of validity of Mobile Offshore 
Drilling Unit Safety Certificate. The time between any two inspections must not exceed thirty six) 
months. However, if the Mobile Offshore Drilling Unit when a certificate expires is not in the place in 
which it is to be surveyed, the Director-General may extend the period of validity of the certificate but 
this extension should be granted only for the purpose of allowing the unit to proceed to the place in 
which it is to be surveyed, and then only in cases where it appears proper and reasonable to do so. No 
certificate should be extended for a period longer than three months, and a unit to which an extension 
is granted should not, on its arrival in the place in which it is to be surveyed, be entitled by virtue of 


such extension to leave that place without having a new certificate. When the renewal survey is 


228 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


completed, the new certificate should be valid to a date not exceeding five years from the date of 
expiry of the existing certificate before the extension was granted. 
In-water survey may be permitted in lieu of dry-docking for intermediate docking for all Mobile 
Offshore Drilling Units subject to the approval of the Director-General, provided that the mobile 
offshore drilling unit comply with International Maritime Organisation Resolution A.l207(34) — 
Survey Guidelines under the Harmonized System of Survey and Certification (HSSC), 2025 — as well 
as the applicable requirements of the classification society or recognised organisation as notified by 
the Central Government ; 

(f) radio station surveys within three months before or after the anniversary date of the mobile offshore 
drilling unit Code certificate, a periodical survey shall be carried out by the jurisdictional Mercantile 
Marine Department or recognised organisation; and 


(g) an additional survey as the occasion arises and as decided by the Director-General. 


2) The jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation notified by the 
Central Government may issue a Mobile Offshore Drilling Unit Safety Certificate for a maximum 
period of five years from the date of expiry of existing full term certificate or endorse the 
certificate upon satisfactory completion of the applicable survey. However, the validity of Mobile 
Offshore Drilling Unit Safety Certificate may be limited to the validity of the unit’s class 
certificate, or it may coincide with the survey cycle of the classification society or recognised 
organisation, as applicable while carrying initial survey of the mobile offshore drilling unit when 
it is newly constructed or change of flag. 

3) The owners or managers of the mobile offshore drilling unit shall apply to the jurisdictional Mercantile 
Marine Department or recognised organisation notified by the Central Government to conduct the required 
surveys within the designated window period. However, if the owners or managers intend to commence 
and complete the surveys outside the specified window period, or if the surveys are overdue, they must 
obtain prior concurrence from the Director-General for conducting such overdue surveys beyond the 
allowed timeframe. 

4) For the intermediate survey, the owner or manager of the mobile offshore drilling unit shall submit an 
application along with the requisite fee to the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department under whose 
jurisdiction the Unit is operating, provided that the survey and certification of the Unit are carried out 
exclusively by the recognised organisation. Upon satisfactory completion of the survey, Mercantile Marine 
Department may endorse the certificate for intermediate inspection of Mobile Offshore Drilling Unit 
Safety Certificate. In the case the mobile offshore drilling unit exclusively operating in foreign offshore oil 
fields during the entire window period, the owner or manager may directly approach the recognised 
organisation for conducting the intermediate inspection. The intermediate verification must be carried out 
between the second and third anniversary date of the Mobile Offshore Drilling Unit Safety Certificate. 

5) In the case of self-propelled Mobile Offshore Drilling Units (MODUs), the requirements relating to survey 
and certification under the International Convention for the Safety of Life at Sea (SOLAS), 974, as 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 229 


amended, shall apply in addition to the requirements of the Mobile Offshore Drilling Unit Code, 989 or 
2009, as applicable. 


4. Surveys and certification under other International Maritime Organisation Conventions: 


The survey and certification of the mobile offshore drilling unit under other International Maritime 
Organisation conventions—such as International Convention for Prevention of Pollution from Ships 
(MARPOL), International Tonnage Convention, 969 (ITC-969), Loadline Convention, Ballast Water 
Management Convention, Anti-Fouling System Convention and any other convention as applicable, must 


be carried out in accordance with the provisions of those respective conventions, as applicable. 


(]) an initial survey before the unit is put in service or before the certificate is issued for first 
time; 

(2) an annual survey within three months before or after each anniversary date of the certificate; 

(3) an intermediate survey within three months before or after the second or third anniversary date of 


the certificate; 

(4) a renewal survey at intervals specified by the Director-General but not exceeding five years except 
where the renewal survey is completed within three months before the expiry date of the existing 
certificate, the new certificate shall be valid from the date of completion of the renewal survey to a 
date not exceeding five years from the date of expiry of the existing certificate or the renewal 
survey is completed after the expiry date of the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be 
valid from the date of completion of renewal survey to a date not exceeding five years from the 
date of expiry of the existing certificate, or the renewal survey is completed more than three 
months before the expiry date of the existing certificate, the new certificate should be valid from 
the date of completion of the renewal survey to a date not exceeding five years from the date of 
completion of the renewal survey, and; 

(5) an additional survey as occasion arises and as decided by the Director-General. 

(6) All ship owners or managers must submit applications for all applicable statutory surveys to the 
jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or the recognised organisation notified by the Central 
Government, well within the designated window period. This is to ensure timely planning, 
execution of surveys, and issuance of statutory certificates. 

(7) The initial or renewal Load line survey and issuance of full term Load Line Certificate shall be 
undertaken by a recognised organisation notified by the Central Government for the said purpose. 
The annual Load line surveys can be undertaken by any one of the recognised organisations of the 
Central Government. On satisfactory completion of annual Load line surveys, the recognised 
organisation may endorse the load line certificate towards completion of annual Load line survey. 
Further, the concerned recognised organisation should ensure that the reports of the annual Load 
line surveys are expeditiously forwarded to an authorized recognised organisation by the Director- 


General through an order for updating the records of Load line surveys. 


5. Approval of plans, drawings, manuals and booklets for issuance of statutory certificates: 


230 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


(J) Any plan, drawing, manual or booklet towards the issuance of statutory certification of an existing 
mobile offshore drilling unit undergoing change of flag to India registry, shall be deemed to be 
approved by the Director-General, if the same is duly approved by any recognised organisation 
notified by the Central Government, on behalf of any other Flag State Administration. No further 
approval from the Director-General or any other recognised organisation is required in these cases, 
provided no substantial deviation from the applicable national or international instruments, 
affecting the safety of lives or protection of the environment is reported during the statutory 
inspection and certification services rendered by the concerned recognised organisation. The 
Registering authority shall appropriately record the reasons as above, for deeming any plan, 
drawing, manual or booklet as approved by the Director-General. Owners shall ensure such 
drawings or booklets or manuals are legible and in English language and contain all the 
information required as per relevant instrument. 

(2) Recognised organisations undertaking statutory surveys for a mobile offshore drilling unit change 
of flag to the Indian registry shall confirm that all relevant plans, drawings, booklets, and manuals 
are duly approved by a recognised organisation notified by the Central Government. These 
documents must generally comply with the applicable rules under the Act, international 
conventions, codes, and any guidelines or notices issued by the International Maritime 
Organisation and the Director-General in this regard. 

Such compliance shall be ensured prior to the issuance of the respective statutory certificates. Any 
non-compliance or deficiency identified must be communicated in writing for prompt rectification, 
and in no case shall the rectification period exceed three months. 

(3) In the event of a mobile offshore drilling unit change of flag, the procedure outlined above shall be 
followed for the approval of plans, drawings, manuals, and booklets related to load lines. Upon 
satisfactory completion of the required surveys, the recognised organisation notified by the Central 
Government for the said purpose shall issue the full-term Load Line Certificate. 

(4) For initial assignment of load line for new construction mobile offshore drilling unit, the 
recognised organisation notified by the Central Government for the said purpose shall approve all 
the plans, drawings, booklets, manuals etc. as required and applicable, and carry out the initial 
surveys towards issuance of full term load line certificate. 

(5) Any plan, drawing, or booklet related to a newly constructed mobile offshore drilling unit must be 
approved by a recognised organisation notified by the Central Government, specifically for the 
purpose of statutory certification services. Each approved document must clearly display the name, 
designation, signature, and date of the authorised person who has certified it. If any of these details 
are missing or illegible, the local representative of the concerned recognised organisation shall be 


responsible for certifying the veracity of such documents. 


(6) In the event of a change of flag, the recognised organisation undertaking the statutory surveys shall 


issue a provisional International Tonnage Certificate (ITC) valid for six months, based on the 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 


234 


(7) 


International Tonnage Certificate issued under the previous flag. The recognised organisation shall 
forward this provisional certificate, along with the tonnage computations carried out by any 
recognised organisation notified by the Central Government, to the Registrar of the vessel for 
issuance of a permanent International Tonnage Certificate. However, if the tonnage computations 
were not carried out by a recognised organisation notified by the Central Government and not in 
accordance with the provisions of the International Tonnage Convention, 969 (ITC-969), the 
recognised organisation authorised by the Director-General through an order shall undertake a 
fresh tonnage measurement in accordance with the provisions of the Merchant Shipping (Tonnage 
Measurement of Vessels) Rules, 2026. The revised tonnage computations shall be submitted to the 
Registrar of the vessel within the validity period of the provisional International Tonnage 


Certificate for the purpose of issuing the permanent International Tonnage Certificate. 


For a new construction mobile offshore drilling unit, the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine 
Department or recognised organisation authorised by the Director-General through an order, shall 
carry out the tonnage measurement and computations in accordance with the provisions of the 
Merchant Shipping (Tonnage Measurement of Vessels) Rules, 2026. Upon completion, the tonnage 
details shall be submitted to the Registrar of the vessel for the issuance of the Permanent 


International Tonnage Certificate (ITC) or Indian Tonnage Certificate, as applicable. 


6. Extension of dry docking, mandatory statutory surveys and certificates: 


(I) 


(2) 


No statutory certificate shall be extended beyond its validity for the purpose of commercial 
operation. However, if a mobile offshore drilling unit is not in the port where the survey is to be 
conducted at the time the certificate expires, the Director-General may extend the validity of the 
certificate for a maximum period of three months. This extension is granted only to allow the 
vessel to complete its voyage to the port where the survey will be carried out. 

However, in exceptional circumstances, the extension of the renewal or docking survey shall be 
granted only in accordance with the provisions of the Safety of Life at Sea (SOLAS), Load Line, 
International Convention for Prevention of pollution from Ships (MARPOL) or any other 
applicable Convention, as well as the applicable rules under the Act. Such applications shall be 
submitted by the top management under the International Safety Management (ISM) Code or by 
the Designated Person Ashore (DPA) of the company. 


7. Exemptions: 


() 


When acquiring mobile offshore drilling unit that are not newly built, any exemptions granted by 
the previous Flag State Administration in accordance with the relevant international conventions 
shall remain valid for a period of three months, or until the original expiration date of such 
exemptions under the previous flag, whichever occurs earlier and the recognised organisation shall 


issue exemption accordingly with an intimation to Director-General. 


232 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


If an extension of the validity of such exemptions is required beyond this period, the company 
shall apply to the Director-General within the current validity period. The application must be 
accompanied by the recommendations of the recognised organisation and include the applicable 


fee as prescribed in the schedule. 


For the continued validity of existing exemptions granted by the previous flag and in accordance 
with the relevant international conventions, the recognised organisation shall forward the details to 
the Director-General for concurrence, enabling the Director-General or recognised organisation to 
issue the exemptions under Indian flag. 

(2) For newly built mobile offshore drilling unit, any exemption to be granted in accordance with the 
relevant international conventions for the issuance of statutory certificates shall require the owners 
or managers of such vessels to submit an application to the Director-General. The application shall 
be accompanied by the recognised organisation recommendations and the applicable fee as 
prescribed in the schedule. Such requests will be considered in line with the provisions of the 
relevant international conventions. 


8. Formats of certificates and forms: 


l. All convention certificates shall be issued in the format specified by the organisation. 
2. All other certificates and forms which are not specified in this Schedule shall be issued in the 


format prescribed in the respective rules.

Schedule-V 


[See sub-rule () of rule 4] 
Survey and Certification of Indian Coastal Vessels 


l. Applicability: 


The provisions contained in this schedule shall apply to the survey and certification of Indian Coastal 


Vessels of such type, size and operational limits, as may be specified by the Director-General through an 


order under section 30 of the Merchant Shipping Act, 2025 (24 of 2025). 


Provided that such vessels are engaged exclusively in operations within coastal waters. 


(I) 


(2) 


Surveys and certification: 


The hull, closing appliances, machinery, life-saving appliances, pollution prevention equipment 


and measures, radio installations and other equipment should be subject to the surveys specified 


below: 
(i) an initial survey, shall be carried out before the coastal vessel is put into service; 
(ii) an annual survey shall be carried out within three months before or after each anniversary 


date of the Indian Coastal Vessel Safety Certificate; 

().. था intermediate survey shall be carried out within three months before or after the second 
or third anniversary date of the Indian Coastal Vessel Safety Certificate; 

(iv) A renewal survey shall be carried out at intervals specified by the Director-General, not 
exceeding five years. If the renewal survey is completed within three months before the 
expiry of the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for five years from the 
date of expiry of the existing certificate. If the renewal survey is completed after the expiry 
of the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for five years from the date of 
expiry of the existing certificate, or the renewal survey is completed more than three 
months before the expiry date of the existing certificate, the new certificate should be valid 
from the date of completion of the renewal survey to a date not exceeding five years from 
the date of completion of the renewal survey, and; 

(v) an additional survey, to be conducted as occasion may arise, and such times and in such 
manner as may be determined by the Director-General. 

A minimum of two inspections of the outside of the Coastal vessel’s bottom should be carried out 

during the five year period of validity of the Indian Coastal Vessel Safety Certificate provided the 

interval between any two such inspections shall not exceed thirty-six months. In case of coastal 
vessels less than fifteen years of age, the intermediate dry docking survey may be carried out as an 
in-water survey using closed-circuit television(CCTV) In-water surveys may also be permitted in 
lieu of dry-docking for intermediate docking for coastal vessels of more than fifteen years of age, 
subject to the approval of the Director-General and provided that the vessel comply with 

International Maritime Organisation Resolution A.l207(34) — Survey Guidelines under the 

Harmonized System of Survey and Certification (HSSC), 2025 — as well as the applicable 

requirements of the classification society or recognised organisation (RO) as notified by the 


Central Government. 


234 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


(3) Owners or managers of coastal vessel shall apply to the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine 
Department or a recognised organisation notified by the Central Government to conduct 
surveys within the designated window period. If the owner or manager is intend to 
commence and complete the surveys outside the window period, or if the statutory surveys 
are overdue, shall obtain prior approval from the Director-General before commencing 


such surveys. 


(4) Owners or managers of coastal vessels shall present the vessel to the jurisdictional Mercantile 
Marine Department for the intermediate survey of the Indian Coastal Vessel Safety Certificate and 
the Indian Coastal Vessel Pollution Prevention Certificate at a convenient port, within the specified 
window period. This requirement applies where the vessel's initial or renewal surveys were 
conducted, and the certificates were issued, by a recognised organisation notified by the Central 
Government. 

(5) The Indian Coastal Vessel Safety Certificate or the Indian Coastal Vessel Pollution Prevention 
Certificate, as applicable, shall be issued or endorsed by the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine 
Department or recognised organisation upon satisfactory completion of the relevant survey. 

(6) The validity of the Indian Coastal Vessel Safety Certificate and Indian Coastal Vessel Pollution 
Certificate may be restricted to validity of the full term class certificate or in coincide with the 
class survey cycle, as applicable. 

(7) Boilers are required to be surveyed periodically, with a minimum of two internal inspections 
during each five-year special survey cycle; and the interval between any two such internal 
inspections shall not exceed thirty-six months. One of the internal inspection in conjunction with 
Initial or renewal of Indian Coastal Vessel Safety Certificate Survey and the other with 
Intermediate Indian Costal Vessel Safety Certificate Survey. The external or visual inspections 
annually in conjunction with annual or intermediate or renewal or initial survey. 

3. Approval of plans, drawings, manuals and booklets for issuance of statutory certificates: 

(]) Any plan, drawing, manual or booklet towards the issuance of statutory certification of an existing 
vessel undergoing change of flag to Indian registry, shall be deemed to have been approved by the 
Director-General if such document has been duly approved by any recognised organisation notified 
by the Central Government, on behalf of any other Flag State Administration. No further approval 
from the Director-General or any other recognised organisation is required in these cases, provided 
no substantial deviation from the applicable national instruments, affecting the safety of lives or 
protection of the environment is reported during the statutory inspection and certification services 
rendered by the concerned RO. The Registering authority shall appropriately record the reasons as 
above, for deeming any plan, drawing, manual or booklets approved by the Director-General. The 
owners shall ensure such drawings or booklets or manuals are legible and in English language and 
contain all the information required as per relevant instrument. 

(2) Recognised organisations undertaking statutory surveys for a vessel's change of flag to the Indian 


registry shall confirm that all relevant plans, drawings, booklets, and manuals are duly approved by 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण

(3) 


(4) 


(5)

a recognised organisation notified by the Central Government. These documents shall in generally 
comply with the applicable rules under the Act, and any guidelines or notices issued by the 
Director-General in this regard. Such compliance shall be ensured prior to the issuance of the 
respective statutory certificates. Any non-compliance or deficiency identified must be 
communicated in writing for prompt rectification, and in no case shall the rectification period 
exceed three months. 

Any plan, drawing, manual or booklet related to a newly constructed vessel must be approved by a 
recognised organisation authorised by the Director-General through an order, specifically for the 
purpose of statutory certification services. Each approved document must clearly display the name, 
designation, signature, and date of the authorised person who has certified it. If any of these details 
are missing or illegible, the local representative of the concerned recognised organisation shall be 


responsible for certifying the authenticity and veracity of such documents. 


In the event of a change of flag, the recognised organisation undertaking the statutory surveys shall 
issue a Provisional International Tonnage Certificate (ITC) valid for six months, based on the 
International Tonnage Certificate issued under the previous flag. The recognised organisation shall 
forward this provisional certificate, along with the tonnage computations carried out by any 
recognised organisation notified by the Central Government, to the Registrar of the vessel for 
issuance of a permanent International Tonnage Certificate. However, if the tonnage computations 
are not carried out by a recognised organisation and not in accordance with the provisions of the 
International Tonnage Convention, 969 (ITC-969), the recognised organisation authorised by the 
Director-General through an order shall undertake a fresh tonnage measurement in accordance 
with the provisions of the Merchant Shipping (Tonnage Measurement of Vessels) Rules, 2026. The 
revised tonnage computations shall be submitted to the Registrar of the vessel within the validity 
period of the provisional International Tonnage Certificate for the purpose of issuing the 


permanent International Tonnage Certificate. 


For a new construction vessel, the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or recognised 
organisation authorised by the Director-General through an order, shall carry out the tonnage 
measurement and computations in accordance with the provisions of the Merchant Shipping 
(Tonnage Measurement of Vessels) Rules, 2026. Upon completion, the tonnage details shall be 
submitted to the Registrar of the vessel for the issuance of the permanent International Tonnage 


Certificate (ITC) or Indian Tonnage Certificate, as applicable. 


Extension of dry docking, tail shaft, mandatory statutory surveys and certificates: 


No statutory certificate shall be extended beyond its validity for the purpose of commercial 
operation. However, if a vessel is not in the port where the survey is to be conducted at the time the 


certificate expires, the Director-General may extend the validity of the certificate for a maximum 


236 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


period of three months. This extension is granted only to allow the vessel to complete its voyage to 
the port where the survey will be carried out. 

(2) In exceptional circumstances, the extension of the renewal or docking survey shall be granted only 
in accordance with the provisions of the applicable rules. Such applications shall be submitted by 
the top management under the Safety Management or by the designated person ashore (DPA) of 
the company. 

(3) The Director-General may not grant surveys extension for commercial reasons or due to 
insufficient advance planning. To request an extension, vessel owners must provide clear 
documentary evidence demonstrating diligent and timely planning efforts. Generic 
correspondence, such as routine dry-dock booking letters, will not suffice. If the Director-General 
is satisfied that the owners took all necessary steps to secure a dry-dock but were unable to do so 


for reasons beyond their control, a one-month extension may be considered. This is subject to: 


(a) Satisfactory afloat surveys by the classification society, and 
(b) Class certification of the vessel’s seaworthiness for the extension period. 
(c) In exceptional cases, an additional up to two months may be granted following: 
(i) An underwater closed-circuit television survey in the presence of Class or 


recognised organisation, 
(ii) Review of progress of other afloat surveys, and 
(॥) Evidence of continued efforts to obtain dry-docking, either in India or abroad. 
(4) Approval of an extension is not guaranteed, and the Director-General reserves the right to deny 
requests if planning is deemed inadequate or if the vessel's condition is unsatisfactory. 
(5) In order to facilitate the expeditious processing of survey extensions, Indian Coastal vessel that are 
not more than twenty years of age, shall be eligible for this process, subject to the following 


conditions: 


(a) the vessel must not have any pending or overdue class/statutory surveys and no outstanding class 


recommendations or conditions affecting certification; 


(b) the vessel’s performance and maintenance history during the current certification period must be 
satisfactory; 
(c) the vessel must not have undergone any significant modification or damage repair that would 


require additional survey; 


(d) the vessel must have no more than three Condition of Classes (CoCs), and none of those Condition 


of Class should have been previously extended; 


(e) there should be no major Non-Conformities in the last two consecutive Domestic Document of 


Compliance (DDOC) audits of the company; 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 237 


(f) the company should not have requested extensions for Flag State Inspection (FSI) deficiencies and 


the vessel is not detained; 
(g) the vessel must not have been involved in any marine casualty; and 
(h) the maximum permissible period between two dockings shall not exceed thirty-six months; 


The owners or managers of such vessels shall submit an application, along with all supporting documents 
that comply with the above-mentioned requirements, to a recognised organisation authorised by the 
Director-General through an order. The recognised organisation shall consider the extension of surveys — 
including the docking survey and tail shaft survey — for a period of one month and forward its report to 
the Director-General for consideration of a further extension of up to two months, but not exceeding a total 
of three months from the date of expiry of the existing full-term certificates. However, the extension of 
surveys beyond one month, the vessel required to undergo under water inspection of hull and its 
appendages, propeller and rudder by a satisfactory closed-circuit television inspection by the recognised 


organisation. 


5. Formats of certificates and forms: 


All certificates and forms shall be issued in the format prescribed in the respective rules, order or 


notice issued by the Director-General.

Schedule - VI 


[See sub-rule (l) of rule 4] 
Survey and Certification of Indian River-Sea Vessels 


Applicability: 

The provisions contained in this schedule shall apply to the survey and certification of Indian River Sea 
Vessels of such type, size and operational limits, as may be specified by the Director-General through an 
order under section 30] of the Merchant Shipping Act, 2025 (24 of 2025). 

Provided that such vessels are engaged exclusively in operations within coastal waters. 

Surveys and Certification: 

(i) The hull, closing appliances, machinery, life-saving appliances, pollution prevention equipment 


and measures, radio installations and other equipment should be subject to the surveys specified 


below: 
(i) an initial survey shall be carried out before the River-Sea Vessel is put in service; 
(ii) a renewal survey shall be carried out at intervals specified by the Director-General, not 


exceeding five years. If the renewal survey is completed within three months before the 
expiry of the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for five years from the 
date of expiry of the existing certificate. If the renewal survey is completed after the expiry 
of the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for five years from the date of 
expiry of the existing certificate, or the renewal survey is completed more than three 
months before the expiry date of the existing certificate, the new certificate should be valid 
from the date of completion of the renewal survey to a date not exceeding five years from 
the date of completion of the renewal survey, and; 

(iii) an intermediate survey shall be carried out within three months before or after the second 
or third anniversary date of the Indian River- Sea Vessel Safety Certificate; 

(iv) an annual survey shall be carried out within three months before or after each anniversary 
date of the Indian River- Sea Vessel Safety Certificate; and 

(v) an additional survey, to be conducted as occasion may arise, and at such times and in such 
manner as may be determined by the Director-General. 

(2) A minimum of two inspections of the outside of the River-Sea vessel’s bottom shall be carried out 
during the five year period of validity of the Indian River-Sea Vessel Safety Certificate provided 
that the interval between any two such inspections shall not exceed thirty-six months. In case of 
River- Sea vessels less than fifteen years of age, the intermediate dry docking survey may be an in- 


water survey using closed circuit television. 


(3) Owners or managers of River-Sea Vessel shall apply to the jurisdictional Mercantile 
Marine Department or a recognised organisation (RO) notified by the Central Government 
to conduct surveys within the designated window period. If owners or managers intend to 


commence or complete the surveys outside the window period, or if the statutory surveys

Schedule-VII 


[See sub-rule () of rule 4] 
ISM audits of Vessels and companies 


Applicability: 


(i) This schedule is applicable to the Indian vessels as follows:- 


(0) Passenger vessels including passenger high-speed crafts, of any size; 


(ii) Oil tankers, chemical tankers, gas carriers, bulk carriers, and high-speed cargo crafts of 
500 gross tonnage or more; 


(ili) Other cargo vessels and mobile offshore drilling units of 500 gross tonnage or more; 
(iv) Domestic cargo vessels of 500 gross tonnage or more. 


(2) Ships carrying dry cargo in bulk but which do not meet the International Maritime Organisation 
Res. Maritime Safety Committee.277 (85) definition for a “Bulk Carrier” are to be typed as “Other 
Cargo Ships.” 


Company responsibilities and authorities: 


Company: 

(]) The “Top Management” for the purpose of this schedule means the highest level of management of 
the Company, who is authorised to provide decisions necessary to implement the Company’s 
objective for the effective implementation of Safety Management System (SMS) on its fleet and in 
this case, refers to the Chairman, Managing Director, President, Chief Executive Officer or 


equivalent, who represents the Board of Directors of the Company. 


(2) Any organisation, including ship management companies, assuming such responsibilities as the 
Company under the International Safety Management (ISM) Code shall have sufficient 
wherewithal and capabilities, such as technical, financial and human resource, readily at its 
disposal for the effective implementation of the Safety Management System at all relevant levels in 
the Company, and shall ensure that adequate resources and shore based support are provided at all 
times and promptly to the vessels under its safety management. Reference: International Maritime 
Organisation Circular Maritime Safety Committee-Marine Environment Protection 
Committee.7/Circ.8 (Revised Guidelines for the operational implementation of the International 


Safety Management Code by Companies). 


(3) Every company who is holding a Document of Compliance (DOC) issued by the Director-General 
or intending to obtain the same, except for the vessels owned and operated directly by the 
Government owned companies or organisations, shall submit a ‘Self Declaration’ from top 


management (i.e Form ISM-I3 or ISM-I4), along with applicable enclosures, while applying for 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 243 


2.25 


2.3. 


any Document of Compliance audit to the International Safety Management cell of the Directorate 


General of Maritime Administration (DGMA). 


Technical management: 


(I) 


(2) 


(3) 


(4) 


The organisation vested with the technical management of vessels may also assume the role as the 
‘company’ for the purpose of the implementation of the International Safety Management Code. 
Thus, any company taking over such responsibilities, shall be duty-bound for the effective 
implementation of the Safety Management System on such vessels and therefore shall be capable 
of demonstrating that the company has sufficient wherewithal and delegated authority for the said 
purpose. In order to confirm the above, both the registered owner of the vessel and the prospective 
International Safety Management manager shall be required to submit a joint undertaking to the 
Director-General in the prescribed format (ISM Form-0). 

If the company has outsourced a part function such as crew management either from the ship 
owner or from a crew management company, the company (Document of Compliance holder) 
remains responsible for all such delegated functions and must enter into an agreement between 
owner of the vessel or the crew managers, as the case may be. The agreement must ensure that the 
Master, officers and crew appointed by the owner or crew manager shall report to the Company 
(Document of Compliance holder) for all Safety Management System matters, shall comply with 
the company instructions and have a direct link in all matters with the shore based designated 
person of the "company." In case of crew mangers, other than the owners, the firm shall be holding 
a valid licence as per the applicable rules made under the Act dealing with recruitment and 
placement of seafarers. 

Notwithstanding any such part or full delegation of International Safety Management 
responsibilities, the owner of the vessel, shall have the ultimate responsibility in accordance with 
section 28 of the Merchant Shipping Act, 2025 (24 of 2025). 

Further, the company, in all such cases of delegation of International Safety Management functions 
should periodically verify and confirm that all those undertaking delegated International Safety 
Management related tasks are acting in conformity with the Company's responsibilities under the 
International Safety Management Code. (reference: International Maritime Organisation 


Resolution Maritime Safety Committee 353(92)). 


Change of management: 


() 


While a ship owner has the right to delegate International Safety Management responsibilities to 
any qualified management company of their choice, frequent changes in management can 
undermine the stability of the Safety Management System. Such instability can compromise vessel 
safety and may create legal, technical, and social issues for the Director-General. Therefore, ship 
owners must exercise great care and diligence when choosing an International Safety Management 
manager. If a change in management becomes unavoidable due to exceptional circumstances, the 


vessel’s registered owner must submit a formal application to the Director-General at least seven 


244 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


(2) 


(3) 


working days before the intended change. This application must be accompanied by a declaration 


from the owner, completed in ISM Form-0. 


The Company planning to take over the management of a vessel from another management shall 

have a valid Document of Compliance for the type of vessel intending to take over and is advised 

to undertake a detailed pre-takeover inspection of the vessel and shall ensure the following prior to 
offering the vessel for the Interim Safety Management Certificate audit :- 

(i) that the vessel is in possession of all valid statutory certificates and shall be free of any 
statutory note or any over-due Conditions of Class (CoCs) as per International Association 
of Classification Societies (IACS) Recommendation 98; 

(ii) that the new joining ship-staff undergo required familiarization on vessel’s lay out, 
loading or discharging, mooring, navigation and engine room procedures or operation with 
the existing staff; 

(iii) that a familiarisation training is imparted to the ship staff on the Safety Management 
System of the company with special emphasis on the onboard safety or emergency systems 
and procedures. 

In case any management company, other than the owner, opts to discontinue the management of a 

vessel, the “Company” may proceed so, in accordance with the bilateral agreement with the 

owners, under intimation to Director-General. However, in all such cases, the management 
company shall be under obligation to continue to discharge its responsibilities as the Company for 

a period of three months from the date of such intimation or until alternate arrangement for safe 

management of the vessel is ensured by the owner, whichever is earlier. If the owner fails to make 

alternate arrangement for the safe management of the vessel within the above mentioned three 
months, the registered owner of the vessel shall automatically be deemed to be the ‘company’ for 


the purpose of the International Safety Management code and the responsibilities there-under. 


2.4. Technical Management of vessels from outside India: 


() 


(2) 


Companies intending to manage Indian Flag vessels from outside India, but do not have registered 
office in India or not registered under Indian Companies Act are eligible for applying for the Indian 
Document of Compliance, provided the Technical Management Company abroad has a liasoning 
arrangement with an Indian registered company and hold a Document of Compliance for the "ship 
type" issued by the maritime administration or by one of the recognised organisations notified by 
the Central Government, on behalf of a maritime administration where the Technical Management 


Company is located. 


The Technical Management Company is required to submit its application in the prescribed format 
along with a duly notarized (in original) undertaking from self, liaison office in India and from the 
ship owner, stating the following.- 


(i) In case of legal disputes, the jurisdiction would be of an Indian Court of Law; 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 245 


2.5. 


(3) 


(ii) In case of any eventuality with respect to safety of the vessel, personnel and environment, 
the ship owner is equally responsible to deal with the above, and will not cite non- 
cooperation by the Technical Manager as a reason for not discharging his responsibility. 

(॥) The Director-General has the right to withdraw the Document of Compliance at its 
discretion, by assigning the reasons which led to such withdrawal. 

(iv) The Technical Management Company is required to take cognizance of the Merchant 
Shipping Act, 2025 (24 of 2025) and Merchant Shipping (Safety Management) Rules, 
2026 and guidelines issued from time to time with respect to the International Safety 
Management system. 

The Technical Management Company is required to be responsible for and arrange for the conduct 

of the interim and initial audit of its office by the Director-General for the purpose of Document of 


Compliance certification and thereafter for the subsequent periodical audits as applicable. 


Change of name or office premises of the company or change of flag or ownership or registry of the 


vessel: 


(I) 


(2) 


(3) 


In case any company intends to change the name of the company or shift the offices premise due to 
operational reasons to a location other than that mentioned in the existing Document of 
Compliance, the company may make a formal application in this regard to the Director-General, in 
the company’s new letterhead, along with the copy of the certificate of incorporation issued by the 
Registrar of Companies or the registered document for the new office premise, as the case may be. 
After incorporating necessary amendments to the Safety Management System documents, 
Company may offer the new office for an Additional verification audit by the Administration, 
along with applicable fees in this regard. On receipt of satisfactory report from the audit team, the 
Director-General will issue new Document of Compliance and Safety Management Certificate, as 
the case may be, with the new address/name, but with validity of the existing certificates. 

In both the cases above, the additional audit referred therein can be clubbed with the annual or 
renewal Document of Compliance audits, if feasible, but in any case, the entire process for the 
change of name or address has to be completed within three months from the date of application 


for the same. 


In case of change of flag or ownership or closure of registry of a vessel holding Safety 
Management Certificate, the company has to promptly intimate the same and surrender the original 
Safety Management Certificate of the vessel to the Director-General, in any case within fifteen 


days. 


Safety Management System: 


() 


The International Safety Management Code requires that companies shall develop, implement and 
maintain a safety management system (SMS) which includes functional requirements as listed in 
section |.4 of the International Safety Management Code and, inter-alia, shall: 

(i) designate a person or persons with direct access to the highest level of management who 


should monitor the safe operation of each vessel; 


246 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


(2) 


(ii) 


(iii) 


(iv) 


(v) 


(vi) 


ensure that adequate resources and shore-based support are provided to enable the 
designated person or persons to carry out their functions ; 

define and document the master's responsibility with regard to reviewing the safety 
management system and reporting its deficiencies to the shore-based management ; 
establish procedures for reporting and analysis of non-conformities, accidents and 
hazardous occurrences; 

periodically evaluate the effectiveness of, and when needed, review of the safety 
management system ; and 

perform internal audits to verify whether safety management activities comply with the 


requirements of the safety management system. 


Safety Management System Manuals: Every company has to develop their own Safety 


Management System manuals considering the type of vessel and nature of operation of the vessel 


taking the following into consideration:- 


(i) 


(ii) 


(iii) 


(iv) 


(v) 


The apex manual of the company is to be referred to as “Safety Management Manual” and 
is required to be in compliance with the various elements of the International Safety 
Management Code, as amended. The manual must contain Policies, objectives, 
organisation structure, responsibilities and authorities of relevant personnel, 
communication level and identification, risk assessment and control of processes of the 
company and on board the vessel with respect to International Safety Management Code. 
The manual shall be so prepared that frequent amendment to the manual is avoided. 

In the event of any other operational manuals, instructions, checklists, forms etc. 
constituting a part of the Safety Management System, the apex manual shall contain cross 


references to all those documents. 


The company Safety Management System Manuals shall address the applicable codes, 
recommendations, guidelines and other safety and security-related non-mandatory 
instruments as per International Maritime Organisation Circular Maritime Safety 
Committee. /Circ.l37l, as amended. Also, company Safety Management System Manuals 
shall address basic elements of Shipboard Occupational Health and Safety Programme 
(SOHSP) and Guidance of Fatigue Mitigation and Management i.e. Maritime Safety 
Committee/Circ. 04 etc. 

The apex Safety Management Manual can have provision to indicate reference to other 
relevant manuals or documents viz. Environmental, Energy Management, Health, Quality 
Manual, Functional Guidelines etc. but not the other way round. 

Companies may opt for Integrated Safety Management system covering International 
Safety Management, International Organisation for Standardisation (ISO), Health Safety 
and Environment (HSE), Operational Health and Safety Assessment Series (OHSAS) etc. 


to suit their commercial requirements subject to covering all the clauses of International 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 247 


Safety Management Code in serial order with clear reference of respective clause in apex 
safety management manual. 

(vi) The Company’s Safety Management System shall comply with the requirements of 
International Maritime Organisation Resolution Maritime Safety Committee. 428 (98) 


concerning Maritime Cyber Risk Management within Safety Management Systems. 


Designated Person Ashore (DPA): Role, responsibility and qualification of designated person ashore shall 


be broadly guided by the International Maritime Organisation Circular Maritime Safety Committee- 


Marine Environment Protection Committee.7/Circ.6 “Guidance on the Qualifications, Training and 


Experience necessary for undertaking the role of the Designated Person under the provisions of the 


International Safety Management code” and as a minimum shall, in addition, comply with the following by 


all Indian Document of Compliance holders:- 


(I) 


(2) 


(3) 


(4) 


Role and responsibility of designated person ashore: Designated person ashore is the key person 
to ensure effective implementation of the company's Safety Management System and requires to be 
based ashore whose influence and responsibilities should significantly affect the development and 
implementation of a safety culture within the Company and hence is required to be placed at a 
reasonably higher level (senior management position) in the company's organisation chart. The 
designated person ashore is required to effectively influence the mind-set, attitudes and behavior of 
subordinate shore staff to enhance support of vessel operations, as well as acting as a key link in 


the safety management chain reporting directly to the highest-level management of his company. 


Qualification of designated person ashore: Every company shall employ a designated person 
ashore on a regular and continuous basis, in full compliance with the rule 6 of the Merchant 
Shipping (Safety Management) Rules, 2026. 

Change of designated person ashore: 

(i) In case of any change of designated person ashore, the Company shall ensure that the new 
designated person ashore meets the applicable qualification requirements as mentioned 
above, and shall promptly report the same to the Director-General in the prescribed format 
(Form ISM-II), along with self-attested copies of Certificate of Competency and 
Continuous Discharge Certificate of the new designated person ashore. 

(ii) If the designated person ashore unilaterally resigns or disassociates with the “company”, 
he may proceed as so per the term of his employment, but under prompt intimation to the 
Director-General. If the company fails to appoint suitable designated person ashore, the 
Document of Compliance of the “Company” shall automatically become null and void. 

Alternate Designated Person Ashore (ADPA): Every Company having more than three vessels 

under its management shall designate an Alternate Designated Person (ADPA), to discharge the 

functions of the Designated Person Ashore in latter’s absence or in similar emergency situations, 
whose qualification and training is in accordance with rule 6 of the Merchant Shipping (Safety 


Management) Rules, 2026. 


248 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


Document of Compliance (DOC): A Document of Compliance (DOC) is issued to a Company when the 
shore side aspects of the Safety Management System are found to fully comply with the requirements of 
the International Safety Management Code. The Document of Compliance is specific to the vessel type(s) 
operated by the Company and for which the SMS is implemented at the time of the audit. A copy of the 
relevant Document of Compliance should be placed on board each of the company’s vessels, which may be 
accepted as evidence that the Company’s shore-side management structure complies with the requirements 
of the Code. 

(l) Application process: 

(i) Any Company intending to obtain a Document of Compliance may make a formal application to 
that effect to the Director-General, as follows:- 

(a) Company request letter on company letter head along with requisite fee. 

(b) Background of the Company. 

(c) Organisation structure giving the qualification & experience of personnel including 
Designated Person Ashore qualification & experience along with the self-attested copies of 
Certificate of Competency and Continuous Discharge Certificate. 

(d) Types of vessels for which Document of Compliance is being applied. 

(e) A hard and soft copy of company Safety Management System document (Apex) in 
compliance with International Safety Management Code for the required vessel type. 

(f) International Maritime Organisation Identification Number for the company issued by 
International Maritime Organisation or their nominated agency. 

(g) Copy of registered document for the office premise. 

(h) Copy of Certificate of Incorporation issued by Registrar of Companies along with copy of 
Memorandum/ Articles of Association. 

(i) Declaration of the company (i.e. Form ISM-3 or ISM-4). 

(ii) After preliminary scrutiny of Company’s application, the Company should submit its Safety 
Management System manual to an auditor nominated by the Director-General for the purpose. The 
auditor, on satisfactory completion of preliminary scrutiny, shall endorse and return the manual to 
the company and forward appropriate recommendation to the Director-General within the period 
prescribed in the nomination letter. 

(2) Interim Document of Compliance certification: 

(i) An Interim Document of Compliance may be issued to facilitate initial implementation of 
the Code when- 

(a) a company is newly established; or 
(b) new vessel types are to be added to an existing Document of Compliance 

(ii) Towards the interim Document of Compliance certification, the Director-General, on 
satisfactory completion of preliminary scrutiny of the Safety Management System 
manuals, may nominate an audit team to the Company’s premises to verify that the 
Company has capabilities as mentioned in para 2.& 2.2 of this schedule, for the effective 


implementation of the International Safety Management Code, for the identified vessel 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] 


भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 249 


(3) 


(iii) 


(iv) 


type(s). The Company will need to prove that measures are in place to implement the full 
requirements of the International Safety Management Code within the period of validity of 
the Interim Document of Compliance. 

On receiving audit report from the auditor, the Company shall first address the non- 
conformities, if any, and then submit application to the Director-General, with requisite fee 
for issuance of Interim Document of Compliance. An interim Document of Compliance 
shall be issued for a maximum period of twelve months from the date of completion of the 
audit or for a shorter period as deemed fit and recommended by the audit team. 

The Document of Compliance audit will take place at the company’s principal place of 
business. If a Company operates from more than one location where different safety 
management functions are performed, then, all these ‘Branch offices’ need to be offered 


for audit. 


Initial, Annual, Renewal and Additional Document of Compliance audits: 


(i) 


(ii) 


(iii) 


Every company should make application for Initial, Annual, Renewal or Additional 
Document of Compliance audit, along with Form ISM-03 and applicable fees, at least one 
month prior to the proposed audit date to facilitate timely nomination of the auditor by the 
Director-General. However, it is desired that for the annual or renewal Document of 
Compliance audits, the company may submit the application on commencement of the 
window-period. 

While applying for any such audit, the company should be capable to demonstrate, with 
relevant supporting records, the satisfactory functioning of the Safety Management System 
on board a vessel of the applicable type for at least three months since the preceding audit 
by the Director-General, as applicable. The records in these cases may include, but not 
limited to the statutory, classification, personnel, maintenance and other relevant Safety 
Management System records of all the vessels operating under the respective Document of 
Compliance. 

On receipt of audit report from the lead auditor, the Company shall first address the non- 
conformities, if any to be addressed immediately, and then submit the application to the 
Director-General with requisite fee for the issuance of full term Document of Compliance, 


as the case may be. 


Safety Management Certificate (SMC) Certification: Subsequent to a successful audit, a Safety 


Management Certificate (SMC) will be issued to each individual vessel provided that the company holds a 


valid Document of Compliance for the applicable type of vessel. A copy of each Safety Management 


Certificate should be retained in the company’s office records, the original being placed on board and filed 


along with all other statutory certificates.

Interim Safety Management Certificate Audits: 


(i) 


For Passenger or Special Trade Passenger vessels, the company which is holding passenger 


vessel Document of Compliance shall make an application to the Director-General along

THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


(ii) 


with Forms ISM-0l, ISM-02, ISM-0, ISM-] and requisite fee. Forms ISM-0l & ISM-02 
are not required, if the owner and the manager of the vessel are the same. After satisfactory 
scrutiny of application, Director-General, will nominate an audit team for Interim SMC 
audit. On receiving audit report from the auditor, the Company shall first address the non- 
conformities, if any, and then submit application with requisite fee for the issuance of 
Interim SMC. 

For Cargo vessels and mobile offshore drilling units, the company which is holding 
document of compliance for the respective type of vessel may directly offer the vessel to 
the recognised organisation authorised by the Director-General by an order for the interim 
audit. However, the Company shall submit duly filled Forms ISM-0l, ISM-02, ISM-l0 
and ISM-I] to Director-General, who shall then issue necessary authorization to the 
recognised organisation for undertaking the audit. Forms ISM-0] & ISM-02 are not 
required, if the owner and the manager of the vessel are the same. On satisfactory 
completion of the audit, recognised organisation may issue Interim Safety Management 
Certificate to the vessel for six months and forward report to the Director-General within 


fifteen days. 


(2) Initial, intermediate, and renewal Safety Management Certificate Audits: 


(i) 


(ii) 


(iii) 


For Passenger or Special Trade Passenger Vessels, company may make the application to 
the Director-General along with requisite fee and form ISM-03 for initial, intermediate and 
renewal Safety Management Certificate audit, at least one month prior to the proposed 
audit date to facilitate timely nomination of the auditor by the Director-General. In case the 
Director-General nominated auditor is not available on the date of the audit, the Principal 
Officer (PO) of the district where the audit is planned may nominate a suitably qualified 
alternate auditor for the audit. On receipt of the satisfactory audit report, Director-General 
will issue Safety Management Certificate subject to payment of fees for the certificate. 

For Cargo Vessels and mobile offshore drilling units, the company may directly offer the 
vessel to the recognised organisation authorised by the Director-General through an order 
for the initial and renewal Safety Management Certificate audits. After satisfactory 
completion of the audit, recognised organisation may issue short term certificate valid for 
five months or may extend existing Safety Management Certificate for five months in case 
of Renewal safety management certificate audit subject to audit completed within three 
months validity of the certificate. On receipt of satisfactory audit report from recognised 
organisation, the Director-General will issue full term Safety Management Certificate 
subject to payment of fees and satisfactory closures of non-conformities, if any. 

For intermediate Safety Management Certificate audits of all vessels in India, the company 
may make application along with requisite fee & form ISM-03 to the Principal Officer 
(PO) of the region where vessel is operating or audit is planned. On satisfactory 
completion of the audit, the Principal Officer or auditor may forward the audit report to the 


Director-General within ten working days. In case of vessels exclusively trading in

Additional Safety Management Certificate Audits: 


(i) 


(ii) 


(iii) 


In case an Additional SMC audit is imposed by any recognised organisation or the Flag 
State surveyor, the concerned authority shall inform the same to the Director-General 
promptly with clear reasons for the action. The Director-General may review the case and 
advise any recognised organisation or Administration auditor to undertake the additional 
audit based on the merit of the case. 

In instances where the additional safety management system assessment of a vessel has 
been imposed by any Port State Control (PSC) authority, recognised organisation may 
board the said vessel at the earliest opportunity to confirm that immediate corrective 
measures have been taken to facilitate prompt release of the vessel from the PSC 
intervention and thereafter shall forward a detailed report to the Director-General, with 
clear recommendation for long term measures to avoid recurrence, viz. additional Safety 
Management Certificate audit, Document of Compliance audit, Flag State inspection etc., 
as necessary. 

In both the cases above, i.e., as stated in clauses (i) and (ii) of sub-paragraph (3) of 
paragraph 6 , it shall be obligatory on the Company to promptly report the matter to the 
Director-General and to recognised organisation to facilitate additional Safety 
Management Certificate audit as necessary. However, this does not absolve the concerned 
recognised organisation from its obligations to fulfill the Procedural Requirements (PRs) 


of the Indian Association of Classification Societies, as applicable. 


Procedures for auditors for carrying out Interim Safety Management Certificate audits: 


(i) 


(ii) 


Auditor must verify that all the requirements of International Safety Management code are 
complied with during interim Safety Management Certificate audit for implementation of 
the company Safety Management System except verification of Safety Management 
System records. 

Auditor must verify that the vessel in possession of all applicable statutory certificates, 
including of valid Document of Compliance or Interim Document of Compliance (DOC) 
of the Company for the applicable vessel type, and that the vessel is free from any 


statutory note or overdue Conditions of Class (CoCs). 


252 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


(5) 


(iii) 


(iv) 


(v) 


Auditor must verify that the safe manning is provided onboard as per the minimum safe 
manning document issued by the Administration and that all shipboard personnel are well 
aware of their role and responsibility in performing their functions as laid down in the 
company Safety Management System and have clear understanding of the company's 
policy in implementation of the safety and pollution prevention aspects. Auditor may 
undertake realistic emergency preparedness drills and ensure that they are effectively 
implementable. 

Auditor must verify that all shipboard important working drawings & documents are in 
English and clearly readable. In the event of documents not being in English or 
incomprehensible or illegible, a limited interim Safety Management Certificate may be 
issued for a maximum period of six months in order to facilitate the company to place 
onboard all drawings documents which are legible and in english. 

In the event any major issues have been observed to be existing in the vessel’s 
performance or its critical machinery or equipment during the audit or from records 
available on board, the auditor shall report it as non-conformity or major non- conformity 
as appropriate and the recognised organisation shall then consult the Director-General to 


decide on the further course of action as needed. 


Reinstatement of Safety Management Certificate when window period lapses due to prolonged 


layup/docking: 


(i) 


(ii) 


Intermediate Safety Management Certificate audits: If for unavoidable reasons, vessels 
have to be laid up during the window-period of the intermediate Safety Management 
Certificate audits and the audit window closes during the said layup or docking period, the 
existing Safety Management Certificate becomes invalid. In such cases, the recognised 
organisation may undertake the “Intermediate Safety Management Certificate audit”, under 
intimation to the Director-General, beyond the window period without any further 
approval from Director-General, when the vessel is not in dry dock or lay-up and the audit 
scope would be to the extent of “initial” audit. On satisfactory completion of the said audit, 
the Safety Management Certificate, if decided to be validated, would be issued with an 
endorsement as “Validity restored after satisfactory conduct of audit as applicable for 
Initial audit”. The validated Safety Management Certificate would be valid from the date 
of completion of the said initial audit for a period not exceeding the original date of expiry 
of existing Safety Management Certificate. 


Renewal Safety Management Certificate audits: If Renewal Safety Management 
Certificate audit could not be completed before the expiry of existing Safety Management 
Certificate, the recognised organisation can undertake the “Renewal SMC audit” beyond 
the window-period without any further approval from Director-General, provided the 
vessel was laid-up after the expiry of the certificate. However, the new Safety Management 
Certificate would be valid from the date of completion of the renewal audit to a date not 
exceeding five years from the date of expiry of the previous Safety Management 
Certificate. 


Non Conformity and Major Non Conformity: 


(l) 


The Company shall submit the detailed Corrective Action (CA) along with Root Cause Analysis 
(RCA) and Preventive Actions (PA) proposed with regard to each of the Non-Conformities (NCs) 
raised during the audit to the concerned Lead Auditor (LA) for his acceptance within the time- 
limit prescribed by him, but in any case within three months from the date of audit. In case the 
Company fails to satisfactorily address the non conformities within three months from the date of 
audit, necessary concurrence for its closure needs to be obtained from the Director-General. 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 253 


(2) All major non-conformities are to be dealt with immediately as per Maritime Safety Committee 
Circularl059- Marine Environment Protection Committee Circular 40].

safety and pollution prevention aspects of the operation of each ship and to ensure that adequate resources and 
shore based support are applied, as required.

I, ------------------------- presently serving as ----------------------- of --------------- , hereby state that I represent the 
Top Management (Board of Directors) of the Company and is authorised to affirm as follows:-. 
l. That the Top Management (Board of Directors) of M/s -------- , recognizes that by virtue of being the holder 
of the Document of Compliance No----------- issued by the Government of India on ---------- , M/s------------- 


- is the “Company” within the meaning of rule 6(h) of the Merchant Shipping (Safety Management) Rules, 
2026 as amended and the organisation vested with the responsibility and the authority to ensure the safe 
operation of the ships under its management, and, 


2 That the Board acknowledge that as the ‘company’ defined under the International Safety Management code 
responsible for the safe operation of ships, we are duty-bound to ensure that the company has sufficient 
wherewithal, both technically and financially, to provide adequate and prompt shore based support to the 
vessels under its management at all times, and, 


3. That the Board takes cognizance of the requirements relating to the Company’s responsibility towards 
ensuring adequate resources under International Safety Management Code as defined in paragraph 3.3 of the 
Resolution A.74l(/8) as amended by Maritime Safety Committee.l04(73), Maritime Safety 
Committee.!79(79), Maritime Safety Committee.95(80) and Maritime Safety Committee.273(85), and, 


4. That towards compliance with the aforementioned requirements of the International Safety Management 
Code, the Company has provided and will continue to provide adequate resources in terms of skilled 
manpower, material and services for safe operation and pollution prevention in compliance with all 
applicable rules and regulations. In support of this contention we are submitting herewith statement for the 
preceding financial year as vetted by our bonafide Chartered Accountants, and, 


5. That the Company has paid, on time, necessary dues to the providers of all resources including seafarers, 
suppliers of stores, spares, bunkers etc., and other service providers including recognised organisations, and, 


6. That we are aware that the failure from the company to provide timely resources and services to a vessel can 
render her unseaworthy and unsafe within the meaning of section !27, 278 and 307 of the Merchant 
Shipping Act, 2025 (24 of 2025) as amended and that the Company is liable to be proceeded against, as per 
the applicable statues, for such lapses.

INTERNATIONAL SAFETY MANAGEMENT (ISM) CODE 
DECLARATION FROM THE COMPANY 
I presently serving as CEO/President of M/s. (the Company) hereby 
state that I represent the Company and am authorised to affirm as follows: 
l. That M/s , recognizes that by virtue of being the holder of the Document of 
Compliance No issued by the Government of India on M/s. is the “Company” 


within the meaning of rule 2(g) of the Merchant Shipping (Safety Management) Rules, 2026 as amended and is 
vested with the responsibility and the authority to ensure the safe operation of the ships under its management, and 


2. That the Company acknowledges that as the ‘company’ defined under the ISM code responsible for safe 
operation of ships, we are duty bound to ensure that the company has sufficient wherewithal, both technically and 
financially to provide adequate and prompt shore based support to the vessels under its management at all times, and 


3. That the Company takes cognisance of the requirements relating to Company’s responsibilities towards ensuring 
adequate resources under the International Safety Management Code as defined in para 3.3 of the Resolution 
A.74(/8) as amended by Maritime Safety Committee. 04(73), Maritime Safety Committee. 79(79), Maritime 
Safety Committee. 95(80) and Maritime Safety Committee. 273(85), and 


4. That towards compliance with the aforementioned requirements of the International Safety Management Code, the 
Company has provided and will continue to provide prompt and adequate resources in terms of skilled manpower 
material and services for safe operation and pollution prevention in compliance with all applicable rules and 
regulations. In support of this contention we are submitting herewith the annual report for the preceding financial 
year as vetted by our statutory auditors, and 


5. Save and except disputed dues and commercial arrangements, the Company has paid, on time, necessary and 
legitimate dues to the providers of all services including seafarers, suppliers of stores, spares, bunkers etc. and other 
service providers including recognised organisations, and 


6. That the Company is aware that the failure of the Company to provide timely resources and services to a vessel 
can render her unseaworthy and unsafe within the meaning of relevant sections of the Merchant Shipping Act, 2025 
(24 of 2025) as amended and that the Company is liable to be proceeded against as per the applicable statutes, for 
such lapses. 


7. The Board of Directors of the Company have been kept informed of the contents of this Declaration.
