THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


Schedule-VHI 
[See sub-rule () of rule 4] 


Domestic Safety Management System Audits of Vessels and Companies 


Applicability.- The provisions relating to the Domestic Safety Management (DSM) shall apply to the 

Indian Domestic Vessel as follows:- 

(J) to Coastal Vessels of 500 Gross Tonnage and above. 

(2) to River-Sea Vessel of Category Type-3 and Type-4 of 500 Gross Tonnage and above or carrying 
more than twelve passengers; and 

(3) to any other category of domestic vessel as decided by the Director-General. 

Company Responsibilities and Authorities: 

(i) The “Jop Management” for the purpose of this schedule means the highest level of management of 
the Company, who is authorised to provide decisions necessary to implement the Company’s 
objective for the effective implementation of Safety Management System (SMS) on its fleet and in 
this case, refers to the Chairman, Managing Director, President, Chief Executive Officer or 
equivalent, who represents the Board of Directors of the Company. 

(2) Any organisation, including ship management companies, assuming such responsibilities as the 
Company under the Merchant Shipping (Safety Management) Rules, 2026, shall have sufficient 
wherewithal and capabilities, viz. technical, financial and human resource, readily at its disposal for 
the effective implementation of the Safety Management System at all relevant levels in the 
Company, and shall ensure that adequate resources and shore based support are provided at all times 
and promptly to the vessels under its management. 

The Safety Management System: The owner, operator, or manager who has assumed responsibility for the 

operation of a Coastal Vessel, a River -Sea Vessel or any other category of domestic vessel as decided by the 

Director-General, and who thereby undertakes the duties and responsibilities set forth in the Merchant 

Shipping (Safety Management) Rules, 2026 (hereinafter referred to as the "Company")—shall develop and 

implement a Safety Management System (SMS) that meets the requirements of the said Rules. 

Designated Person Ashore (DPA): 

(i) The Company shall nominate an employee as designated person ashore, who is having appropriate 
knowledge on domestic Vessels operation and having direct access to top management. However, 
every company shall employ a designated person ashore on a regular and continuous basis, in full 


compliance with the rule 6 of the Merchant Shipping (Safety Management) Rules, 2026. 


(2) In case of any change of designated person ashore, the Company shall ensure that the new 
designated person ashore meets the applicable qualification requirements as mentioned above and 
shall report the same to the Director-General or recognised organisation (RO) authorised by the 
Director-General through an order along with copies of Certificate of Competency and Continuous 


Discharge Certificate, Education Qualifications and experience of the new designated person ashore. 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 273 


5. Assessment for Compliance: 

(i) The company responsible for the management and operation of Indian domestic vessels, such as 
Coastal Vessels, River-Sea Vessels, or any other category of domestic vessel as decided by the 
Director-General, shall submit its Safety Management System to the Director-General or a 
recognised organisation authorised by the Director-General for document review. 

(2) Upon satisfactory completion of the document review, an initial audit of the Company’s shore-based 
safety management system and associated documentation shall be conducted by an auditor(s) 
nominated by the Director-General or recognised organisation authorised by the Director-General on 
a mutually convenient date. 

(3) Upon satisfactory completion of the initial audit, the Director-General or the recognised organisation 
shall issue a full-term Domestic Document of Compliance (DDOC), valid for a maximum period of 
five years. This Domestic Document of Compliance shall apply to domestic vessels such as coastal 
vessels, River-sea vessels, or any other category of domestic vessel as decided by the Director- 
General. 

(4) An intermediate verification audit of the Company’s shore-based safety management arrangements 
and associated documentation shall be conducted by an auditor(s) nominated by the Director- 
General or recognised organisation authorised by the Director-General, within a window of two and 
a half years (plus or minus six months) following the issuance of the full-term Domestic Document 
of Compliance (DDOC). 

(5) A renewal audit of the company’s safety management arrangements and related documentation 
ashore shall be carried out by an auditor(s) nominated by the Director-General or recognised 
organisation authorised by the Director-General on or before with a window of 3 months prior 
expiry of the full-term Domestic Document of Compliance. Upon satisfactory completion of the 
renewal audit, a fresh full-term Domestic Document of Compliance, valid for a maximum period of 
five years from the date of expiry of the existing Domestic Document of Compliance, shall be issued 
to the Company. 

(6) The initial assessment of the company’s safety management arrangements and _ related 
documentation on board domestic vessels shall be carried out by the surveyors of the Mercantile 
Marine Department or recognised organisation authorised by the Director-General. This assessment 
shall take place during the first survey immediately following the issuance of the full-term 
Document of Compliance (DDOC). However, it is mandatory for the owner, manager, or operator to 
be in possession of a valid Domestic Document of Compliance certificate in order to complete the 
Initial, Annual, Intermediate, or Renewal Survey of the Indian Coastal Vessel Safety Certificate or 
Indian River-Sea Vessel Safety Certificate or any other safety certificate issued to any other category 
vessel as decided by the Director-General. 

(7) The assessment of the Company’s safety management arrangements and related documentation on 
board Coastal Vessels, River -Sea Vessels or any other category vessel shall be conducted by the 
Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation authorised by the Director-General. This 


assessment shall take place during the Annual, Intermediate, or Renewal Survey of the Indian 


274 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


Coastal Vessel Safety Certificate or the Indian River-Sea Vessel Safety Certificate or any other 
safety certificate issued to any other category vessel as decided by the Director-General. 

(8) In the event that the Company intends to relocate its office premises for operational reasons to a 
location different from that stated in the existing Domestic Document of Compliance, a formal 
application must be submitted to the Director-General or recognised organisation notified by the 
Director-General. This application should be made on the Company’s new letterhead and must 
include a copy of the registered document pertaining to the new office premises. Following this, the 
Company should update the necessary information in the Safety Management System documents. 
Once these amendments have been made, the Company may offer the new premises for an 
additional verification audit by the Director-General or recognised organisation, along with payment 
of the applicable fees. Upon receiving a satisfactory report from the audit team, the Director-General 
or recognised organisation will issue a revised Domestic Document of Compliance reflecting the 
new office address. The validity of the revised Domestic Document of Compliance will remain the 
same as that of the existing certificate. 

(9) If the Company wishes to change its name as mentioned in the current Domestic Document of 
Compliance, it may submit a formal application to the Director-General or recognised organisation 
authorised by the Director-General. This application should be on the Company’s new letterhead and 
must include a copy of the certificate of incorporation issued by the Registrar of Companies, along 
with any other corroborating documents, if applicable. Upon satisfactory scrutiny of the application, 
the Director-General or recognised organisation will issue a fresh Domestic Document of 
Compliance reflecting the Company’s new name, while retaining the existing validity period. 

(l0) | Acompany holding a valid Document of Compliance (DOC) for the relevant vessel type—issued by 
the Director-General or recognised organisation under the Merchant Shipping (Safety Management) 
Rules, 2026 may take responsibility for operating the Type 3 and Type 4 River-Sea Vessels or 
Coastal Vessels or any other category vessels as decided by the Director-General, without needing 


the Domestic Document of Compliance (DDOC). 


Audit requirements for the Document of Compliance certificate will comply with the Merchant 
Shipping (Safety Management) Rules, 2026. However, the audit requirements specific to River-Sea 
Vessels or Coastal Vessels or any other category vessel under the Domestic Safety Management 


System shall follow the provisions outlined in paragraphs 5(6) and 5(7) of this Schedule. 


6. Non Conformity and Major Non Conformity: 
l. The Company shall submit the detailed Corrective Action (CA) along with Root Cause Analysis 
(RCA) and Preventive Actions (PA) proposed with regard to each of the Non-Conformities (NCs) 
raised during the audit to the concerned Lead Auditor (LA) for his acceptance within the time-limit 
prescribed by him, but in any case within three months from the date of audit. In case the Company 
fails to satisfactorily address the non-conformities within three months from the date of audit, 


necessary concurrence for its closure needs to be obtained from the Director-General. 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3(i)] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 275 


2. All major non-conformities must be addressed immediately to prevent any disruption to vessel 
operations. 
7. Domestic Safety Management Forms: The following forms are required to be used by the auditors (i.e., 


Directorate General of Maritime Administration and recognised organisation auditors) and Companies for 
conducting Domestic Document of Compliance Audits and Shipboard Safety Management System 
verification during surveys. The format of these Domestic Safety Management forms are available at the end 


of this schedule for reference.

Schedule-IX 
[See sub-rule () of rule 4] 
International Ship Security and Port Facility (ISPS) Audits of Vessels 


l. Application: 
This schedule shall apply to all Indian Vessels, irrespective of type and tonnage. 
2. Verification and Compliance of Vessels: 

) Types of Verification: 

(a) an initial verification before the ship is put in service, or before the certificate required under these 

tules is issued for the first time, which shall include a complete verification of its security system and any 

associated security equipment covered by the relevant provisions of the Safety Convention, Part-A of the 

International Ship and Port Facility Security Code and the approved ship security plan so as to ensure that 

the security system and any associated security equipment of the ship fully complies with the applicable 

requirements of the Safety Convention and Part-A of the International Ship and Port Facility Security 

Code, and is in satisfactory condition, fit for the service for which the ship is intended; 

(b) renewal verification at intervals specified by the Director-General, but not exceeding five years, 

except as otherwise specified in these rules so as to ensure that the security system and any associated 

security equipment of the ship fully complies with the applicable requirements of Conventions and Part-A 

of the International Ship and Port Facility Security Code and the approved ship security plan and is in 

satisfactory condition, fit for the service for which the ship is intended; 

(c) at least one intermediate verification to be endorsed on the certificate shall be carried out which is to 

take place between the second and third anniversary date of the certificate and shall include inspection of 

the security system and any associated security equipment of the ship so as to ensure that it remains 

satisfactory for the service for which the ship is intended; 

(d) any additional verification as determined by the Director-General. 

2) Ship Security Assessment (SSA): 

(a) As specified in the rules, the Company Security Officer shall ensure that the Ship Security 
Assessment is conducted by individuals possessing the appropriate skills to evaluate the ship's 
security. The Ship Security Assessment may also be carried out by a recognised security organisation 


(RSO) duly recognised by the Central Government. 


(b) The conduct of the Ship Security Assessment shall include an on-scene security survey of the vessel, 


covering the elements and scope specified in the applicable Rules. 

(c) The Ship Security Assessment shall be documented, reviewed, accepted, and retained by the 
Company. 

3) Ship Security Plan (SSP): 


(a) As specified in the Rules, each vessel shall have a Ship Security Plan approved by the Registrar of the 


vessel. A recognised security organisation shall prepare the Ship Security Plan for a specific vessel. 


290 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)]

The Company Security Officer (CSO) shall submit the Ship Security Plan, duly prepared and 
reviewed by the recognised security organisation, to the vessel's Registrar for approval, along with the 
Ship Security Assessment that was conducted. 

Any amendments to an existing approved Ship Security Plan, along with the Security Assessment on 
which the amendments are based, must be submitted to the vessel's registrar for approval. 


Interim verification and certification: 


After the approval of the Ship Security Plan, the company shall approach the jurisdictional Mercantile 
Marine Department or recognised security organisation to carry out an interim verification of the 
vessel to ensure compliance with the code requirements and the rules under the Merchant Shipping 


Act, 2025 (24 of 2025). This interim verification is typically required in the following cases: 


vi) The vessel is newly built. 

vii) The vessel is changing its flag. 

viii) The vessel was not previously under International Ship and Port Facility Security 
compliance. 

ix) The vessel is undergoing major modifications. 

x) The company is newly established and operating the vessel for the first time. 


After verification, the Mercantile Marine Department or recognised security organisation shall issue 
either an Interim International Ship Security Certificate for vessels on International voyage or a Ship 
Security Certificate for vessels including domestic and non-convention vessels, as applicable. This 
certificate shall be valid for a period not exceeding six months and must be issued in the form 
specified in the International Ship and Port Facility Security Code or this schedule, as applicable. 
Initial verification and certification: 

The initial verification shall be conducted by the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or 
recognised security organisation after three months of the implementation and issuance of the Interim 
International Ship Security Certificate or Interim Ship Security Certificate. However, the initial 
verification shall be carried out before the expiry of the Interim International Ship Security Certificate 
or Interim Ship Security Certificate. 

Upon successful completion of the initial verification, the Mercantile Marine Department or 
recognised security organisation shall issue a short term International Ship Security Certificate or Ship 
Security Certificate valid for five months, and forward the verification report to the respective 
registrar of the vessel for issuance of a full-term International Ship Security Certificate or Ship 
Security Certificate, valid for five years from the date of the initial verification, in the form specified 
in the rules or this Schedule, as applicable. 


Intermediate verification, renewal verification and certification: 


For intermediate verification, the company shall submit an application along with the requisite fee to the 


jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department where the vessel is operating or where the audit is planned 


subject to the initial or renewal verification was done by the recognised security organisation. Upon 


satisfactory completion of the audit, Mercantile Marine Department may endorse the certificate for 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 294

intermediate verification and shall forward the audit report to the Director-General within ten working 
days. In the case of vessels exclusively operating in offshore fields or in foreign waters during the entire 
verification window period, the company shall directly approach the recognised security organisation for 
conducting the intermediate audit. The intermediate verification shall be carried out between the second 
and third anniversary date of the International Ship Security Certificate or Ship Security Certificate. 
Renewal verification shall be carried out by the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or 
recognised security organisation with the same scope as the initial verification. This verification must be 
conducted within three months prior to the expiry of the existing International Ship Security Certificate or 
Ship Security Certificate. Upon satisfactory completion of the renewal verification, the Mercantile Marine 
Department or recognised security organisation shall either issue a short-term International Ship Security 
Certificate or Ship Security Certificate, as applicable, or extend the existing certificate for a period of five 
months, in accordance with section 9.3.3 of Part A of the International Ship and Port Facility Security 
Code, as applicable. The Mercantile Marine Department or recognised security organisation shall then 
forward the verification report to the vessel’s Registrar for the issuance of a full-term International Ship 
Security Certificate or Ship Security Certificate, valid for five years from the expiry date of the existing 
certificate in the form specified in the rules or this Schedule, as applicable. 


In cases where intermediate or renewal verification is not completed within the prescribed window period, 
the owners or managers of the vessels concerned shall approach the Director-General for concurrence. 
The Director-General will specify the scope of verification required for reinstating the validity of the 
certificate in relation to the missed intermediate verification. However, if the renewal verification is not 
completed before expiry of the International Ship Security Certificate or Ship Security Certificate, the 
Director-General will issue appropriate directions to the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or 
Recognised Security Organisation, based on the submissions provided by the owners or managers of the 
vessel. 


Additional Verification: 


In the event that an Additional Verification is imposed by the recognised security organisation or the 
Mercantile Marine Department, the concerned authority shall promptly notify the Director-General, 
providing clear reasons for the action. The Director-General may then review the case and, based on its 
merits, and issue appropriate directions to the recognised security organisation or the Mercantile Marine 
Department to carry out the additional audit. 


Verification and compliance of Domestic Vessels: 


The verification and compliance of domestic Vessels shall be conducted concurrently with the Initial or 
Annual or Intermediate or Renewal Surveys of the Safety certificates issued, in accordance with the rules 
framed under the Act, or through an order issued by the Director-General. 


All passenger ships security audits shall be conducted exclusively by the Surveyors of the Directorate 
General of Maritime Administration (DGMA) in compliance with the Code and the applicable rules made 
under the Act. 


3. Formats of certificates and forms: 
All convention certificates shall be issued in the format specified by the organisation. 


All other certificates and forms which are not specified in this Schedule shall be issued in the 
format prescribed in the respective rules.

Schedule-X 
[See sub-rule () of rule 4] 


Maritime Labour Convention Inspections and Certification of Vessels 


l. Applicability: 


(2 


(2 


— 


— 


To all vessels registered under the Act engaged in commercial activities, but does not apply to — 


(a) Vessels which navigate exclusively in the inland waters or waters within, or closely adjacent to, 


sheltered waters or areas where any law for the time being in force relating to ports apply; 


(b) Vessels engaged in fishing activities; 


(c) Traditionally built vessels such as dhows and junks; and 


(d) Vessels of war or naval auxiliaries. 


Inspections and Certification: 


Declaration of Maritime Labour Convention (Part I and IID) & Interim Certification Inspection: 


(a) 


(b) 


(c) 


(d) 


The ship owner is required to submit an application for the issuance of the Declaration of Maritime 
Labour Convention (DMLC) Part-I to the Registrar of the vessel, along with the applicable fees as 
prescribed in the rules under the Act. Upon receipt of the application, the Registrar shall issue the 
Declaration of Maritime Labour Convention Part-I, which outlines the national provisions and 
requirements that implement the Maritime Labour Convention (MLC), 2006. 

The ship owner shall prepare the Declaration of Maritime Labour Convention Part II in accordance 
with national requirements and submit it to the Registrar of the vessel. Upon review, the Registrar 
shall endorse the Declaration of Maritime Labour Convention Part II. 

After receiving Declaration of Maritime Labour Convention Part I and Declaration of Maritime 
Labour Convention Part II, the ship owner shall approach the Jurisdictional Mercantile Marine 
Department or a recognised organisation for an interim inspection of the vessel. 

After an inspection by the Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation, an Interim 
Maritime Labour Certificate (IMLC) shall be issued for a period not exceeding six months under the 


following circumstances: 


(i) For New Vessels on Delivery: When a vessel is newly constructed or entering into service for 


the first time. 


(ii) When a Company Takes Over Responsibility for the Operation of a Vessel: A change in the 


vessel’s management or ownership. 


(iii) When a Vessel is transferred between Flags: The flag state changes, requiring a new 


inspection. 


Initial Inspection and Certification: 


The initial MLC inspection shall be conducted by the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or 


recognised organisation after three months of the implementation and issuance of the Interim Maritime 


Labour Certificate. However, it shall be carried out before the expiry of the Interim Maritime Labour 


Certificate. 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 295 


(0) 


(3) 
(a) 


(b) 


(०) 


(4) 


Upon successful completion of the initial inspection, the Mercantile Marine Department or recognised 
organisation shall endorse the Declaration of Maritime Labour Convention Part II for satisfactory 
inspection and issue a full term Maritime Labour Certificate (MLC) valid for five years from the date of 
the initial inspection, in the form specified in the Merchant Shipping (Maritime Labour) Rules, 2026. 
Intermediate inspection, renewal inspection and certification: 


For intermediate inspection, the company shall submit an application along with the requisite fee to the 
jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department where the vessel is operating or where the inspection is 
planned subject to the initial or renewal inspection done by the recognised organisation and the Maritime 
Labour Certificate (MLC) was issued by the recognised organisation. Upon satisfactory completion of the 
inspection, the Mercantile Marine Department shall endorse the certificate for intermediate inspection and 
shall forward the inspection report to the concerned Registrar of the vessel within ten working days. In 
case the vessel is exclusively operating in offshore fields or in foreign waters during the entire inspection 
window period, the company shall directly approach the recognised organisation for conducting the 
intermediate inspection. The intermediate inspection shall be carried out between the second and third 
anniversary date of the Maritime Labour Certificate (MLC). 


Renewal inspection shall be carried out with the same scope as that of the initial inspection. This 
inspection shall be conducted within three months prior to the expiry of the existing Maritime Labour 
Certificate (MLC). Upon satisfactory completion of the renewal inspection, the Mercantile Marine 
Department or recognised organisation shall issue the Maritime Labour Certificate (MLC) valid for five 
years from the date of expiry of the existing certificate. 


In cases where intermediate or renewal inspection is not completed within the prescribed window period, 
the owners or managers of the vessels concerned must approach the Director-General for concurrence. 
The Director-General will specify the scope of inspection required for reinstating the validity of the 
certificate in relation to the missed intermediate inspection. However, if the renewal inspection is not done 
prior expiry of the Maritime Labour Certificate, the Director-General will issue appropriate directions to 
the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation, based on the submissions 
provided by the owners or managers of the vessel. 


Additional Inspection: 

In the event that an Additional Inspection is imposed by the recognised organisation or the Principal 
Officer, Mercantile Marine Department, the concerned authority shall promptly notify the Director- 
General, providing clear reasons for the action. The Director-General may then review the case and, based 
on its merits, advise the recognised organisation or the Mercantile Marine Department to carry out the 
additional inspection. 


Requirements for - vessels less than five hundred (500) gross tonnage (foreign going and coastal), 
Indian Coastal Vessels and River-Sea Vessels.- 


(a) The Director-General will issue a Declaration of Maritime Labour Compliance - Part I for all vessels 
of less than five hundred gross tonnage, in compliance with the Maritime Labour Convention (MLC), 
2006. This document shall serve as the Declaration of Maritime Labour Convention -I for all vessels 
under five hundred gross tonnage. 

(b) The Declaration of Maritime Labour Compliance — Part II shall be prepared in accordance with the 
requirements outlined in Declaration of Maritime Labour Convention -I. It shall be signed and 
stamped by the owner of the vessel, and submitted to the Mercantile Marine Department along with 
the required financial securities, as specified in the rules. The original copy shall be kept on board the 


vessel.

Compliance verification inspection shall be conducted by the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine 
Department or the recognised organisation, within the scope defined in the rules under the Act, and in 
accordance with the Declaration of Maritime Labour Compliance (DMLC). 

Upon satisfactory completion of the inspection, the Mercantile Marine Department or recognised 
organisation shall issue a Statement of Compliance valid for five years from the date of the inspection 
in a prescribed format specified in the Rules or as attached to this schedule. 

A minimum of two inspections shall be carried out within a five-year period. The interval between 
two inspections shall not exceed three years. Owners or managers of such vessels are required to 
approach the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department for the intermediate inspection within the 
specified window period, provided that initial or renewal inspection was done by the recognised 
organisation and a Statement of Compliance (SOC) was issued by the recognised organisation. In case 
the vessel is exclusively operating in offshore fields or in foreign waters during the entire inspection 
window period, the company shall directly approach the recognised organisation for conducting the 
intermediate inspection. After satisfactory completion of the inspection the Mercantile Marine 
Department or recognised organisation shall endorse the certificate. 

Renewal inspection shall be carried out by the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or 
recognised organisation with the same scope as the initial inspection. This inspection shall be 
conducted within three months prior to the expiry of the existing Statement of Compliance (SoC). 
Upon satisfactory completion of the renewal inspection, the Principal Officer, Mercantile Marine 
Department or recognised organisation shall issue a Statement of Compliance valid for five years 


from the date of expiry of the existing certificate. 


4. Formats of certificates and forms: 


All convention certificates shall be issued in the format specified by the organisation. 
All other certificates and forms which are not specified in this Schedule shall be issued in the 


format prescribed in the respective rules.

Schedule XI 
[See sub-rule () of rule 4] 
Ballast Water Management Convention (BWM Convention) Certifications 


Applicability.- 


The provisions contained in this schedule shall be applicable to every ship of four hundred (400) Gross 
Tonnage or more, to which the provisions of Ballast Water Management Convention are applicable. 


Survey and Certification: 


Ships of four hundred gross tonnage and above to which Ballast Water Management Convention applies, 
excluding floating platforms, floating storage units and Floating Production Storage and Offloading units 


(FPSO), shall be subject to surveys specified below: 


(l) An initial survey before the ship is put in service or before the Certificate is issued for the first time. 
This survey shall verify that the Ballast Water Management plan and any associated structure, equipment, 
systems, fittings, arrangements and material or processes comply fully with the requirements of the 


Convention; 


(2) A renewal survey at intervals specified by the Director-General, but not exceeding five years. If the 
renewal survey is completed within three months before the expiry of the existing certificate, the new 
certificate shall be valid for five years from the date of expiry of the existing certificate. If the renewal 
survey is completed after the expiry of the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for five 
years from the date of expiry of the existing certificate. If the renewal survey is completed three months 
before the expiry of the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for five years from the date 


of completion of renewal survey, and; 


(3) An intermediate survey within three months before or after the second Anniversary date or within 
three months before or after the third Anniversary date of the Certificate, which shall take the place of one 
of the annual survey. The intermediate surveys shall ensure that the equipment, associated systems and 
processes for Ballast Water Management fully comply with the applicable requirements of the Convention 
and are in good working order. Such intermediate surveys shall be endorsed on the Certificate issued 


under the Convention; 


(4) An annual survey within three months before or after each Anniversary date, including a general 
inspection of the structure, any equipment, systems, fittings, arrangements and material or processes 
associated with the Ballast Water Management plan to ensure that they have been maintained in 
accordance with the requirements of the convention and remain satisfactory for the service for which the 
vessel is intended. Such annual surveys shall be endorsed on the Certificate issued under the convention; 


and; 


(5) An additional survey either general or partial, according to the circumstances, shall be made after a 
change, replacement, or significant repair of the structure, equipment, systems, fittings, arrangements and 


material necessary to achieve full compliance with Ballast Water Management Convention. The survey 


300 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


shall be such as to ensure that any such change, replacement, or significant repair has been effectively 
made, so that the ship complies with the requirements of Ballast Water Management_Convention. Such 


surveys shall be endorsed on the Certificate issued under the Convention. 


Explanation.- ‘Significant repair’ means only those repairs or modifications that materially alter the 
approved design, treatment performance, or operational integrity of the Ballast Water Management 
System. Routine maintenance, like-for-like replacement of components, software updates, calibration, or 
minor corrective actions shall not be considered significant repairs. 


(6) If a renewal survey has been completed and a new Certificate cannot be issued or placed on board the 
ship before the expiry date of the existing Certificate, the Recognised Organisation (RO) notified by the 
Central Government may endorse the existing Certificate and such a Certificate shall be accepted as valid 


for a further period which shall not exceed five months from the expiry date. 


(7) If a ship at the time when the certificate expires is not in a port in which it is to be surveyed, the 
Director-General may extend the period of validity of the certificate but this extension shall be granted 
only for the purpose of allowing the ship to complete its voyage to the port in which it is to be surveyed, 
and then only in cases where it appears proper and reasonable to do so. No certificate shall be extended 
for a period longer than three months, and a ship to which such extension is granted shall not, on its 
arrival in the port in which it is to be surveyed, be entitled by virtue of such extension to leave that port 
without having a new Certificate. When the renewal survey is completed, the new certificate shall be valid 
to a date not exceeding five years from the date of expiry of the existing certificate before the extension 


was granted. 


(8) In special circumstances, as determined by the Director-General, a new Certificate need not be dated 
from the date of expiry of the existing certificate as required by paragraph |.2 of this schedule. In these 
special circumstances, the new certificate shall be valid to a date not exceeding five years from the date of 


completion of the renewal survey. 
3. Approval of plans, drawings and manuals for issuance of statutory certificates: 


(J) Any plan, drawing, manual or booklet towards the issuance of International Ballast Water Management 
Certificate of an existing vessel undergoing change of flag to India registry, shall be deemed to be approved by the 
Director-General, if the same is duly approved by any recognised organisation, on behalf of any other Flag State 
Administration and no changes have been made to the same. No further approval from the Director-General or any 
other recognised organisation is required in these cases, provided that the plans, drawings and manuals are in 
conformity with the Merchant Shipping (Control and Management of Vessel’s Ballast Water and Sediments) 
Rules, 2026 and the Ballast Water Management Convention. 


(2) Any plan, drawing, manual or booklet related to a new construction ship must be approved by a recognised 
organisation notified by the Central Government, specifically for the purpose of statutory certification services. 
Each approved document must clearly display the name, designation, signature, and date of the authorised person 
who has certified it. If any of these details are missing or illegible, the local representative of the concerned 


recognised organisation shall be responsible for certifying the veracity of such documents. 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 304 


4. Conduct of surveys: 


All vessel owners or managers must submit an application for the survey such as initial, renewal, annual and 
intermediate to the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or the recognised organisation notified by the 
Central Government, well within the designated window period. This is to ensure timely planning, execution of 
surveys, and issuance of statutory certificates. However, if the owners or managers intend to commence and 
complete the surveys outside the specified window period, or if the surveys are overdue, they must obtain prior 


concurrence from the Director-General for conducting such overdue surveys beyond the allowed timeframe. 
5. Formats of certificates and forms: 
(J) All convention certificates shall be issued in the format specified by the organisation. 


(2) All other certificates and forms shall be issued in the format prescribed in the respective rules. 


302 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


Schedule XII 
[See sub-rule () of rule] 


Anti-Fouling Systems Convention (AFS Convention) Certifications 
. Applicability.- The provisions contained in this schedule shall apply to every vessel- 


(a) having a gross tonnage of four hundred or more; 


(b) engaged in international voyages or plying in coastal waters, and 


(c) having a gross tonnage of less than four hundred but more than twenty four meters in length for the purpose of 


carrying Declaration on anti-fouling system. 
Provided that nothing contained in this clause shall apply to - 


(0) Fixed or Floating platforms; 
(ii) Floating Storage Units (FSUs); and 
(iii) Floating Production Storage and Offloading units (FPSOs). 


2. Survey and Certification.- (]) Vessels of four hundred gross tonnage and above engaged in international 
voyages or plying along the coast of India excluding fixed or floating platforms, floating storage units, and floating 


production storage and offloading units, shall be subject to surveys specified below: 


(a) an initial survey, before the vessel has been put into service, or before the International Anti-Fouling System 


Certificate or, the Indian Anti-Fouling System Certificate is issued for the first time, as the case may be; and 


(b) a survey, when the anti-fouling systems have been changed or replaced, and such change or replacement has 
been endorsed on the International Anti-Fouling System Certificate or the Indian Anti-Fouling System Certificate, 


as the case may be. 


(2) The surveys referred to in sub-rule (.) shall be such as to ensure that the vessel’s anti-fouling system is in 
accordance with and fully complies with the provisions of the Merchant Shipping (Control of Anti-fouling System) 
Rules, 2026 and the Anti-Fouling System Convention. 


(3) The surveys of the vessels referred to in sub-rule () shall be carried out by the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine 
Department (Mercantile Marine Department) or the Recognised Organisation (RO) notified by the Central 
Government. Owners of such vessel’s shall make the application to the concerned well in advance to ensure timely 
planning, execution of surveys, and issuance of International Anti-Fouling System Certificate or Indian Anti- 


Fouling System Certificate, as the case may be, or endorse the certificate. 


(4) The jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or a recognised organisation shall issue the International Anti- 
Fouling System Certificate or the Indian Anti-Fouling System Certificate, as applicable, after satisfactory 
verification of the records and certificates issued under the International Convention on the Control of Harmful 
Anti-Fouling Systems on Ships, 200l (AFS Convention) by the previous Flag State Administration or the 
recognised organisation notified by the Central Government. Such issuance shall be subject to confirmation that the 
vessel complies with the provisions of the Merchant Shipping (Control of Anti-Fouling System) Rules, 2026 and 


the requirements of the Anti-Fouling System Convention at the time of entry into the Indian Register. 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 303 


3. Declaration on anti-fouling system.- 


A vessel of twenty four meters or more in length, but less than four hundred gross tonnage, engaged in international 
voyages (excluding fixed or floating platforms, floating storage units, and floating production storage and off- 
loading units), shall be required to carry a declaration signed by the owner or owner’s authorised agent, and such 
declaration shall be accompanied by appropriate documentation (such as a paint receipt or a contractor invoice) or 
contain appropriate endorsement. The declaration shall be in the format specified in the Merchant Shipping 


(Control of Anti-fouling System) Rules, 2026. 
4. Formats of certificates and forms: 
(J) All convention certificates shall be issued in the format specified by the organisation. 


(2) All other certificates and forms shall be issued in the format prescribed in the respective rules. 


304 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


Schedule-XIII 
[See sub-rule () of rule 4] 
International Code for Ships Operating in Polar Waters (POLAR Code) 
l. Application.- The provisions of this schedule shall apply to all Indian vessels, that are 
intended to proceed to, operate in, or navigate within the Polar water, including; 
(a) Conventional Vessel of gross tonnage five hundred (500) and above; and 
(b) Non-Conventional Vessel of gross tonnage below five hundred (500) 
2. Definitions.- () “Category A vessel” means a vessel designed for operation in polar waters 
in at least medium first-year ice, which may include old ice inclusions. 
(2) “Category B Vessel” means a vessel not included in category A, designed for operation in 
polar waters in at least thin first-year ice, which may include old ice inclusions. 
(3) “Category C Vessel” means a vessel designed to operate in open water or in ice conditions 
less severe than those included in categories A and B. 
(4) “First-year ice” means sea ice of not more than one winter growth developing from young 
ice with thickness from 0.3 m to 2.0 m. 
(5) “Medium first-year ice” means first-year ice of 70 cm to !20 cm thickness. 


(6) “Thin first-year ice” means first-year ice of 30 cm to 70 cm thickness. 


3. Survey and Certification.- (|) Survey and certification of all categories of Polar Vessels 
complying with the requirements of the International Code for Ships Operating in Polar Waters (Polar 
Code) shall follow the same procedures applicable to cargo vessels under the relevant international 
conventions, Codes and guidelines issued by the International Maritime Organisation, and which shall 
be in accordance with the internationally recognised Harmonized System of Survey and Certification 
(HSSC) guidelines, for both convention and non-convention vessels. However, the surveys and 


certification specific to compliance with the Polar Code shall be carried out as detailed below: 


(a) The Polar Ship Certificate shall be issued after an initial or renewal survey to a vessel 
which complies with the requirements of the Polar Code and the certificate shall be 
harmonized with the vessel’s other certificates issued under Safety of Life at Sea (SOLAS) 
convention. 

(b) A renewal survey shall be carried out at intervals specified by the Director-General, not 
exceeding five years. If the renewal survey is completed within three months before the 
expiry of the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for five years from the date 
of expiry of the existing certificate. If the renewal survey is completed after the expiry of the 
existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for five years from the date of expiry of 
the existing certificate. If the renewal survey is completed three months before the expiry of 
the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for five years from the date of 


completion of renewal survey; 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र असाधारण : 305 


(०) an intermediate survey within three months before or after the second anniversary date or 
within three months before or after the third anniversary date of the certificate, which should 
take the place of one of the annual survey; 

(d) an annual survey within three months before or after each anniversary date of the 
certificate; and 

(e) an additional survey as the occasion arises and as decided by the Director-General. 


(2) The jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisations may issue a 
Polar Ship Certificate for a maximum period of five years from the date of expiry of existing 
full term certificate or endorse the certificate upon satisfactory completion of the applicable 
survey. The validity of the Polar Ship Certificate shall be harmonized with the vessel’s other 
certificates issued under Safety of Life at Sea (SOLAS) convention. 


(3) The owners or managers of the Polar Vessel shall apply to the jurisdictional Mercantile 
Marine Department or recognised organisation to conduct the required surveys within the 
designated window period. If the owners or managers intend to commence and complete the 
surveys outside the specified window period, or if the surveys are overdue, shall obtain prior 
concurrence from the Director-General for conducting such overdue surveys beyond the 
allowed timeframe. 

(4) All categories of vessels operating in polar waters shall be assessed for their operational 
limitations, provided with a Polar Water Operational Manual (PWOM) duly approved by any 
recognised organisation, and subsequently surveyed for the issuance of a Polar Ship 
Certificate. 

(5) For category C Cargo vessels, if the operational assessment indicates that no additional 
equipment or structural modifications are needed to meet the Polar Code requirements for the 
intended voyage in Polar Waters, the Mercantile Marine Department or recognised 
organisation shall issue a Polar Ship Certificate based on document verification alone and in 
such cases, the validity of the Polar Ship Certificate shall be confirmed at the next scheduled 
Safety of Life at Sea-related survey. 


(6) The Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation shall verify compliance 
with the pollution-prevention measures specified in Part II A of the Polar Code prior to the 
issuance or endorsement of the Polar Ship Certificate, or any other relevant convention 
certificate, during the annual or intermediate surveys. 


4. Formats of certificates and forms: 


l. All convention certificates shall be issued in the format specified by the organisation. 
2. All other certificates and forms shall be issued in the format prescribed by the respective 


rules.

Schedule - XIV 
[See sub-rule () of rule 4] 


Survey and Certification of Indian River- Sea Passenger Vessels 


Applicability.- The provisions contained in this schedule shall apply to the survey and certification of 
Indian River-Sea Passenger Vessels of such type, size and operational limits, as may be specified by the 
Director-General through an order under section 30! of the Merchant Shipping Act, 2025 (24 of 2025). 
Provided that such vessels are engaged exclusively in operations within coastal waters. 

Surveys and Certification.- (]) The hull, closing appliances, machinery, fire fighting 
appliances, life-saving appliances, navigation equipment, pollution prevention equipment and measures, 
radio installations and other equipment should be subject to the surveys specified below: 

(i) an initial survey shall be carried out before the River- Sea Vessel is put in service; 

(ii) a renewal survey at intervals specified by the Director-General, not exceeding twelve 
months. If the renewal survey is completed within three months before the expiry of the 
existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for twelve months from the date of 
expiry of the existing certificate. If the renewal survey is completed after the expiry of 
the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for twelve months from the date 
of expiry of the existing certificate. If the renewal survey is completed three months 
before the expiry of the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for twelve 
months from the date of completion of renewal survey, and; 

(॥) an additional survey, to be conducted as occasion may arise, and at such times and in 
such manner as may be determined by the Director-General. 

(2) Docking Surveys: 

(i) Any River - Sea Passenger Vessel of less than fifteen years of age, shall undergo a 
minimum of one inspection of the outside of the vessel’s bottom during any five-year 
period in dry-dock. In all such cases, the maximum interval between any two dry-dock 
bottom inspections should not exceed sixty months. 

(ii) Any River - Sea Passenger Vessel of above fifteen years of age but less than thirty years 
of age, shall undergo a minimum of two inspections of the outside of the vessel’s bottom 
during any five-year period in dry-dock. In all such cases, the maximum interval 
between any two dry dock bottom inspections should not exceed thirty six months. 

(iii) Any River - Sea Passenger Vessel of above thirty years of age, shall undergo the 
inspection of the outside of the vessel’s bottom in dry dock during every alternative 
renewal River-Sea Passenger vessel’s Safety Certificate Survey. 

(iv) Inspections of the vessel's bottom is required for the River- Sea Passenger Vessel Safety 
Certificate renewal survey, that are not conducted in dry-dock shall be carried out with 
the vessel afloat using closed circuit television. The bottom inspection to be carried out 
within the allowable time window for the River-sea passenger vessel safety certificate 


renewal survey (i.e. within the three-month time window before the expiry date of the 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र णअसाधार : 307 


() 


(2) 


certificate). Additionally, an inspection of the outside of the vessel's bottom conducted 
afloat is only to be carried out when the conditions are satisfactory and the proper 
equipment and suitably qualified staff is available. Rudder bearing clearances need not 


be taken at the afloat inspections. 


(3) Owners or managers of River - Sea Passenger Vessel shall apply to the jurisdictional 
Mercantile Marine Department or a recognised organisation authorised by the Director- 
General by an order to conduct surveys within the designated window period. If owners 
or managers is intend to commence or complete the surveys outside window period, or if 
the statutory surveys are overdue, the owners or manages of such River- Sea Passenger 
Vessel shall obtain prior approval from the Director-General before the commencement 


of such surveys. 


(4) The validity of the Indian River- Sea Passenger Vessel Safety Certificate may be restricted to 
validity of anniversary date of the class certificate. 

(5) Boilers are required to be surveyed periodically, with a minimum of two internal inspections 
during each five-year special survey cycle; and the interval between any two such internal 
inspections shall not exceed thirty six months. The internal inspection in conjunction with Initial 
or renewal of Indian River- Sea Passenger Vessel Safety Certificate Survey. The external or 
visual inspections annually in conjunction with the renewal survey up to the age of twenty five 
years. Beyond twenty five years of age the boilers are required to undergo internal inspection 
once in a year in conjunction with renewal survey of Indian River- Sea Passenger Vessel Safety 
Certificate. 

Approval of plans, drawings, manuals and booklets for issuance of statutory certificates: 

Any plan, drawing, manual or booklet towards the issuance of statutory certification of an existing river- 

sea passenger vessel undergoing change of flag to Indian registry, shall be accepted provisionally by the 

Director-General, if such document is duly approved in accordance with the applicable convention on 

behalf of any other Flag State Administration by any recognised organisation as notified by the Central 

Government for considering issuance of short term certificates for three months by the jurisdictional 

Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation. The owners or managers of such vessels shall 

submit all the required plans to a recognised organisation authorised by the Director-General through an 

order within the validity of short term certificates for review and submitting the plans to the Director- 

General for review and approval of the same and for issuance of full term certificates by the 

jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation. The Owners shall ensure such 

drawings or booklets or manuals are legible and in English language and contain all the information 


required as per relevant instruments. 


Any plan, drawing, or booklet related to a newly constructed river- sea passenger vessel shall be 


reviewed by a recognised organisation that has been authorised by the Director-General through an order

THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


(3) 


(4) 


(2) 


Nr: 


statutory certification services and forwarding the plans to Director-General for review and approval. 


In the event of a change of flag, the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or recognised 
organisation undertaking the statutory surveys shall issue a Provisional International Tonnage Certificate 
(ITC) valid for six months, based on the International Tonnage Certificate issued under the previous flag. 
The jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation shall forward this 
provisional certificate, along with the tonnage computations carried out by any recognised organisation 
to the Registrar of the vessel for issuance of a permanent International Tonnage Certificate. However, if 
the tonnage computations are not carried out by a recognised organisation and_ the tonnage 
computations are not carried out in accordance with the provisions of the International Tonnage 
Convention, !969 (ITC-969), the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or the recognised 
organisation authorised by the Director-General through an order shall undertake a fresh tonnage 
measurement in accordance with the provisions of the Merchant Shipping (Tonnage Measurement of 
Vessels) Rules, 2026. The revised tonnage computations must be submitted to the Registrar of the vessel 
within the validity period of the Provisional International Tonnage Certificate for the purpose of issuing 


the permanent International Tonnage Certificate. 


For a new construction vessel, the jurisdictional Mercantile Marine Department or the recognised 
organisation authorised by the Director-General through an order, shall carry out the tonnage 
measurement and computations in accordance with the provisions of the Merchant Shipping (Tonnage 
Measurement of Vessels) Rules, 2026. Upon completion, the tonnage details shall be submitted to the 
Registrar of the vessel for the issuance of the Permanent International Tonnage Certificate (ITC) or 
Indian Tonnage Certificate, as applicable. 

Extension of statutory certificates.- (!)No statutory certificate to be extended beyond its validity for 
the purpose of the commercial operation. However, a Vessel at the time when a certificate expires is not 
in a port in which it is to be surveyed, the Director-General may extend the period of validity of the 
certificate for a maximum period of three months only for the purpose of allowing the vessel to complete 
its voyage to the port in which it is to be surveyed. 

The Director-General, may grant an extension of the statutory surveys of any river- sea passenger vessel 
in the public interest, upon the request of the vessel owner or manager. Such an extension shall only be 
considered after due verification, review, and assessment of the ship's condition through a survey, the 
scope of which shall be determined by the Director-General. The survey shall be carried out by a 
surveyor from the Directorate General of Maritime Administration or a recognised organisation 
authorised by the Director-General. Provided that, the statutory certificate shall not be extended beyond 
a maximum period of three months under any circumstances. 


Formats of certificates and forms.- 

All convention certificates shall be issued in the format specified by the organisation. 

All other certificates and forms shall be issued in the format prescribed in the respective rules or 
orders issued by the Director-General. 


309 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


Schedule-XV 
[See sub-rule () of rule 4] 
Survey and Certification of International Code of Safety for Ships Carrying Industrial 
Personnel (IP Code) 


l. Application.- (l) The provisions of the Industrial Personnel Code (hereinafter referred to as 
"the IP Code") shall apply to all cargo vessels of gross tonnage five hundred and above, and 
High Speed Crafts which carry more than twelve industrial personnel respectively. 

(2) Notwithstanding sub-clause (l), the provisions of the Industrial Personnel Code shall, 
insofar as practicable, apply to cargo vessels of gross tonnage below five hundred which carry 
more than twelve industrial personnel in aggregate, comprising passengers, special personnel, 
and industrial personnel. 

Provided that the owner or manager of such vessel referred above shall, where compliance 
with any provision of the Industrial Personnel Code is impracticable, make an application for 
exemption to the Director-General through a recognised organisation or Mercantile Marine 
Department, accompanied by a statement of reasons and supporting documentation justifying 
such exemption. 

Note: The industrial personnel is to be read as the aggregate number of industrial personnel, 
special personnel, and passenger carried on board, where the number of passengers shall not 
exceed twelve. 

2. Definitions.- 

(3) Industrial personnel (IP) means all persons transported or accommodated on board for the 
purpose of offshore industrial activities performed on board other vessels and/or offshore 
facilities. 

(4) Offshore industrial activities mean the construction, maintenance, decommissioning, 
operation or servicing of offshore facilities related, but not limited, to exploration and 
exploitation of resources by the renewable or hydrocarbon energy sectors, aquaculture, 
ocean mining or similar activities. 

3. Survey and Certification.- (l) Survey and certification of vessels that comply with the 
International Code of Safety for Ships Carrying Industrial Personnel (IP Code) shall follow 
the same procedures as those applicable to cargo vessels under all relevant international 
conventions, for convention and non-convention vessels. However, the survey and 
certification specific to compliance with the Industrial Personnel Code shall be conducted as 


detailed below: 


(a) the Industrial Personnel Safety Certificate shall be issued after an initial or renewal survey 


to a vessel which complies with the requirements of the Industrial Personnel Code. 


30 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART II—SEC. 3(i)] 


(b) a renewal survey shall be carried out at intervals specified by the Director-General, not 
exceeding five years. If the renewal survey is completed within three months before the 
expiry of the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for five years from the date 
of expiry of the existing certificate. If the renewal survey is completed after the expiry of the 
existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for five years from the date of expiry of 
the existing certificate. If the renewal survey is completed three months before the expiry of 
the existing certificate, the new certificate shall be valid for five years from the date of 
completion of renewal survey; 

(c) an intermediate survey within three months before or after the second anniversary date or 
within three months before or after the third anniversary date of the certificate, which should 
take the place of one of the annual survey; 

(d) an annual survey within three months before or after each anniversary date of the 
certificate; and 


(e) an additional survey as the occasion arises and as decided by the Director-General. 


(2) Ships that were authorised by the Director-General to carry industrial personnel 
prior to the entry into force of the Industrial Personnel (IP) Code on Olst July 2024 


may continue to carry such personnel subject to the following. 


(a) Existing cargo ships, constructed before Olst July, 2024, shall comply with 
regulations III/l, IIlI/2 (except paragraph 2..7), IV/7, and IV/8 of the Industrial 
Personnel Code at the first intermediate or renewal survey, whichever occurs first, 


after Olst July ,2024. 


(b) Existing high-speed cargo craft, constructed before 0lst July, 2024, shall comply 
with regulations III/I, Il/2 (except paragraph 2..7), V/7, and V/8 of the Industrial 
Personnel Code at the third periodical survey or the first renewal survey, whichever 


occurs first, after Olst July, 2024. 


(c) Existing ships that were not authorised by the Director-General to carry industrial 
personnel prior to 0lst July, 2024 shall be required to fully comply with all applicable 
provisions of the Industrial Personnel Code before being permitted to carry more than 


twelve industrial personnel on board ." 


(3) The Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation notified by the 
Central Government may issue an Industrial Personnel Safety (IPS) Certificate for a 


maximum period of five years from the date of expiry of existing full term certificate 


[भाग ॥-खण्ड 3()] भारत का राजपत्र असाधारण : 344 


or endorse the certificate upon satisfactory completion of the applicable survey. 
However, the validity of the Industrial Personnel Safety Certificate may be limited to 
the validity of the vessel’s class certificate, or it may coincide with the survey cycle of 
the Classification Society or recognised organisation, as applicable while carrying 


initial or renewal survey of the Industrial Personnel vessel. 


(4) The owners or managers of the Industrial Personnel Vessel shall apply to the jurisdictional 
Mercantile Marine Department or recognised organisation notified by the Central 
Government to conduct the required surveys within the designated window period. However, 
if the owners or managers intend to commence and complete the surveys outside the specified 
window period, or if the surveys are overdue, shall obtain prior concurrence from the 


Director-General for conducting such overdue surveys beyond the allowed timeframe. 


Formats of certificates and forms: 


l. All convention certificates shall be issued in the format specified by the 
organisation. 
2. All other certificates and forms shall be issued in the format prescribed in the 


respective rules or orders issued by the Director-General.
